letter of appointment to the workman concerned and the letter 
was being paid on vouchers every week. This witness 
has further stated that even after permanent peon was ap¬ 
pointed at Nagarwada branch, the workman concerned was 
being temporary appointment when the permanent peon used 
to be on leave. He has further stated thai he had ne\er re¬ 
commended to the regional office the name ot the workman 
concerned for being made permanent, He has very cate¬ 
gorically stated that whether it was required the workman 
concerned was being given temporary appointment. The Hank 
has also examined one Sanat Navnitlal at Fx. 21. This 
witness is working as a sectional head in the Bank. He has 
spoken about the guidelines for the appointment of the tem¬ 
porary subordinate staff. As pet the say of this witness, 
if there is ahsenteesim on a large scale or in a case where 
a branch is newly opened, there is a provision to appoint 
temporary subordinate staff till a regular recruit is appoint¬ 
ed. He has further stated that in regard to appointment of 
sub-staff on permanent basis, a list for interview is prepared 
and then as per the requirement first of all the candidates 
are sent for medical test and on their passing the medical 
test they are given appointment letter. He has further 
stated that while sending the candidates for medical test 
they are given a letter in that behalf from the Bank inform¬ 
ing them that on a particular dale and time thev have to 
present themselves before the doctor for their medical test. 
He has categorically stated that only if they have passed 
medical test they are given appointment letters. In his cross- 
examination, a suggestion was made to the witness that the 
letter which is being given to a candidate while he is sent 
fpr medical test is being retained by the doctor after the 
medical test is over, to which he said he had no idea about 
it. But from this it clearly apPears that there is a practice 
of giving a letter to the candidate when he is sent for 
medical teat. 

5. Besides the above oral evidence, the workman con¬ 
cerned has produced a pass-book of his saving bank account 
No. 64/1010. He has also produced a school leaving cer¬ 
tificate by a list. Ex. 14 wherein his date of birth has been 
shown as 3-5-1945. It bIso appears from this certificate that 
he has passed Standard VII, The Bank has also produced 
certain documents by a list, Ex. 10. Anncxure ‘A’ to Ex. 
10 shows the attendance record of the workman concerned 
at the Bank’s main office during the period 1972 to 1975. 
it has also produced a copy of the pass-book pertaining 
to his savings bank account No. 64/1010. The Bank has 
also produced the relevant rules in connection with the 
apponitment of subordinate staff. 

6. Considering the evidence—oral as well as documentary— 
it appears to be the case of the workman concerned that 
he was employed by the Bank first in the year 1969 and he 
was working in the Bank for a considerably long time. How¬ 
ever, there is nothing on the record to show that he entered 
the service of the Bank in the year 1969 beyond the word 
of the workman concerned which is not admitted by the 
Bank. However, it appears that he was taken up first in 
the year 1972 which is supported by the pass-hook as well 
as Bank’s statement, Annexure ’A’ to Ex. Iff. The said 
Anncxure shows that the workman concerned worked for 
31 days in the year 1972, 130 days in the year 1973, 15 
days in the year 1974 and 38 days in 1975. From the evi¬ 
dence produced by the workman concerned as well as the 
Bank it clearly appears that the workman concerned started 
working in the Bank only from May, 1972. It also appears 
that the workman concerned had not worked tegulerly as a 
peon but had worked only intermittently as and when the 
Bank required his services. It further appeare that his date 
of birth is 3-5-1945 and he was given work in the Bank for 
the first time in May, 1972. This means that when he was 
given work for the first time he was about 27 years of age. 
According to the recruitment rules of the Bank as deposed 
to by the sectional head of the Bank, Ex.21, for appointment 
to the post of subordinate staff one should have passed VII 
standard and should be not less than 18 years 
roles have also been produced along with a list Ex. 10 which 
support the say of this witness. Thus, the evidence on record 
clearly shows that when he was temporarily taken up in the 
Bank, he was clearly overage and could not have been ap¬ 
pointed on a regular basis. The case of the Bank is also 
fHaT the Bank had given hin? work only when the Bank re¬ 
quired his services viz., at the intial stage of the opening of 


the Nagarwada Branch when there was no peon in that 
btanch and during the period when any permanent peon was 
on leave. This is also suppoited by the fact that during 
the period 1972 to 1975, as rhe record shows, he was only 
intermittently given work as and when, the Bank required his 
services. 

7. I would, however, first dispose of a preliminary con¬ 
tention taken by Shri Pavaskar representing the Bank. It was 
contended by Shri Pavaskar that the reference made as such 
is had in law inasmuch as reference has been made under 
Sec, 10(lXd) of the Industrial Disputes Act based on the 
assumption that it is a dispute between the Employer and 
the employees. In support of this contention, Shri Pavaskar 
contended that this i s an individual dispute inasmuch as it 
has not been raised by any union and therefore the refer¬ 
ence itself is bad and should be rejected. 1 nave considered 
this contention of Shri Pavaskar and ir my opinion it is 
too technical a point which should not be given weight in 
industrial adjudication. The fact remains that the workman 
concerned was employed by the Bank—may be temporarily 
as and when his services were required and when a regular 
peon was appointed in the Nagarwada branch his services 
were finally terminated which, in my opinion, is required to 
be adjudicated upon in order to see whether the action of 
th e Bank was justified or not. 

8. Coming to the merits of th© case, it is fact that the 
workman concerned was never issued any appointment 
letter nor a letter terminating his services. It clearly appears 
that whenever the Bank required his service temporarily 
due to abscnteeislm or opening of a new branch, etc. In the 
instant case, if is true that the workman concerned was first 
employed by the Bank in the year 1972 and in the following 
years it clearly appears that he was given work for some 
days, a* stated earlier. The workman concerned has never 
worked continuously and at no time for 240 days in a year. 
In his evidencse, the workman concerned has stated that his 
case is one of making him permanent and not for claim¬ 
ing retrenchment compensation on the basis that he was 
retrenched. The case of the workman concerned clearly 
appears to be that he should have been confirmed when he 
has passed the medical examination and when be had put 
in service in the Bank for a considerable time. As far as 
putting in service by him is concerned, it clearlv appears 
that he has not worked for a considerable time nor_e\ra 
for 240 days in any year. It also does not appear that the 
Bank has ever sent him for medical examination. Of course, 
the workman concerned has stated that he was sent for 
medical examinafion as late as on 5th July, 1977 when ho 
was working in the Nagarwada branch. But this does not 
appear to have been proved inasmuch as the Branch Manager 
of the Nagarwada Branch has clearly stated that he him¬ 
self had engaged the workman concerned on purely tem¬ 
porary basis and be had never sent the workman concern¬ 
ed for medical test. It has also come in evidence that 
whenever a workman is sent for medical test he is always 
given a letter addressed to the medical authority who has 
to conduct his medical examination. The workman con¬ 
cerned has categorically admitted that no such letter wa» 
riven to him. From this evidence, it clearly appears that 
it cannot he said that the workman concern was sent for 
medical examination in this case. On the contrary, evi¬ 
dence shows clearly that the Bank had engaged him though 
he was over-age as a Peon, on a purely temporary so to 
sav by wav of stop-gap arrangement as and when such an 
exigency arose. Even when he was engaged at the Nagar- 
wada Brfifich he was engaged till the permanent peon was 
available. Of course, even after the permanent neon was 
posted, he was being given work for some days but that 
was during the absence of that permanent neon. This evi¬ 
dence clearly shows that only interrHttcnflv be was work¬ 
ing in the Bank on a purely temnoran' basis. The Question, 
therefore, that remains to be considered is whether a workman 
who is given termorarv work as above can ho confirmed in 
the Bank’s service. The Bank has examined in this behalf, 
ns stated abovo. its sectional officer who is in know of the 
rules and regulations and he has stated that thev have to 
isk for the names from the Employment Exchange and there 
is more nrocedure to he followed before an aopoinfment 
can be given to a subordinate staff' . Firstly, the workman 
roncemed was overage when he was first given work wbi"h 
goes to show that he could not have been taken up in the 



2325 


3 (ii)] 


w-mm fit 4, i9ss/j?iw u, 1007 


regular service of the Bank. When this was so, there can¬ 
not arise any question of confirming such a pel son in the 
Bank’s service. During the period '972 to 1975, it is clear 
that he was not being given work continuously and when¬ 
ever there was work, he was being taken up afresh. Now 
taking the year 1976 when he was engaged at Nagarwada 
branch after his several terminations before which he has 
never challenged, it is clear that in 1976 he was more than 
25 years of age and he could not hnve been appointed in 
the regular service of the Bank. Shn Kim, the Learned 
Advocate for the workman, concerned, has argued that in 
tegard to the period of employment of the workman con¬ 
cerned at different times the Bank has stated in different 
ways. But that is not so much material inasmuch as it is 
proved beyond doubt that the workrrsan concerned has work¬ 
ed only for some days in different years, never completing 
240 days in any year. Tt is therefore clear that when he 
has not completed 240 days the provisions of Section 25F 
would not be atti acted. As far as the question of making 
him permanent is concerned, as per the rules discussed 
above, there is no question of making him permanent when 
he was not taken up on regular basis and this fact has been 
clearly admitted by the workman concerned himself in the 
last sentence in his cross-examination wherein he has stated: 
“It is true that he was being temporarily employed by the 
Bank hs and when there was work for him". 

9. Shri Kini, however diew my attention to Para 516 of 
the Sastry Award which says - 

“Maintanance of service books—We think that in the 
case of every employee except one who is engaged 
on a part-time basis, whether he is a temporary 
employee, a probationer or a permanent employee, 
a service book should be maintained containing at 
least the following particulars: name, dnte of birth, 
identification marks, entry into service as a tem¬ 
porary employee or probationer, confirmation or 
permanent appointment, pay an such occasions, 
promotion, pay on promotion, disciplinary action, 
if any, taken, any remarks about his efficiency or 
character made bv his superiors, leave taken or 
absence fron> duty, officiating or action appoint¬ 
ment* and deputation, if any, with dates wherever 
possible, resignation or retirement. When adverse 
remarks are made against any employee, a eiat 
thereof should be communicated to him in writing 
with the least possible delav We direct accord¬ 
ingly” 

Relying on the aboye, Shri Kini argued that the workman 
concerned should have been given an appointment letter 
even though, according to the Bank he was given temporary 
appointment. From this, it appears to be the argument of 
Shri Kipi that if Para 516 of the Sastry Award which re¬ 
quires the Bank to maintain service books even of temporary 
employee was applied, he could have proved his say that 
he had put in long service in the Bank and the Bank should 
have absorbed him and made him permanent. Tt is true 
that in this case no service oook of the workman concerned 
appears to have been maintained as nothing of that sort has 
been produced. But the non-compliance of Para 516 of 
the Sastry Award would, in my opinion, not be fatal inas¬ 
much it has no statutory force. At the most non-niainle- 
napee of the service book can be said to be an irregularity. 

T am supported in mv view by the decision of Mr. Justice 
A Varadarajan of the Madras High Court, as he then was. 
reported in 1980-1, T-.L.J. 187. Tt was held in that case that 
Sastry Award as modified by the Desai Award has no statu¬ 
tory force. It Was further held in that decision that the 
ouster of the petitioner in that case who was working in 
leave vacancies in broken period does not amount to re¬ 
trenchment. 

10. Tn regard to the case of the workman concerned that 
he was discremlnated against by the Bank concerned, T do 
not think there is any substance in this contention mas much 
as his ease is altogether a different one as stated above and 
it is not possible for the Bank to confirm him in the circum¬ 
stances or this case. At the same time, it is important to 
note that there is not even an allegation of victimisation on 
the part of the Bank! much less even a suggestion that the 

88 01/85—U 


Bank’s action is malafldc. Thus, the totality of the evi¬ 
dence, if summed up, shows that the workman concerned 
given work on purely temporary basis intermltently as per 
ihe exigencies of the work of the Bank. 

11. T am of the view that when the workman concerned 
was upopinted only for a specific period or for a particular 
work—in the present case the workman concerned was 
employed either in the absence of the permanent peon or 
during the initial period of the opening of the nbw branch 
till a permanent peon is appointed—he has no right to be 
continued thereafter and get work, much less get confirmed. 
This view' of mine is also supported bv a decision of Mr. 
Justice M. M. Ismail of the Madras High Court reported in 
1975-1 L.fi.J. 207. Dealing with such a case in that deci¬ 
sion, Their Lordship observed: 

“Whire the workmen were appointed only for a specific 
period or for a particular work—and as soon as the 
period was over or the work was over, their employ¬ 
ment automatically came to an end and ihee is no 
rule or lavy which contemplates that such employee# 
must be given work again by the employer”. 

From this it clearly appears that the workman concerned had 
no right to get work when a permanetn peon was appointed 
in the Nagarwada Branch or when the permanent peon who 
had taken some leav c had resumed on expiry of his leave. 
Tn my view, therefore, none of the contention raised on be¬ 
half of the workman concerned has any merit. 

12. For the reasons stated above, the action of the manage¬ 
ment of the Bank of Baroda, regional office, Bafoda in ter¬ 
minating the services of Shri H. M. Jadav, sub-staff, from 
9-9-1978 is justified, and th e workman concerned is not 
entitled to any relief. No order at to costs. 

Dated : 8-4-85

central government industrial tribunal- 

CCM-I ABOUR COURT, KANPUR” 


I. D, No. 231 of 1983 

In the riiattc of dispute between : 

Shri Moti fial Kharwal C/o General Secretary, Hlndui- 
tan Commercial Bank Employees Congress 8/75 

Mya Nagar, Kanpur. 

AND 

The General Manager, Hindustan Commercial Bank Ltd,

2326 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 4 , 1985/VAlSAKHA 14, 1907 [Part Ii—S ec. 3(Li)] 


Shri B. G. Agarwal representative—for the management. 

Shri J. C. Dhawan representative—for the workman. 

AWARD 

The Central Government Ministry of Labour vide order 
L-12012/3/83-D.1V (A) dated 9th September, 83 refened 
the following dispute for adjudication : 

“Whether the action of the management of Hindustan 
Commercial bank Limited, Kanpur, in relation to 
their Arya Nagar Branch, Kanpur in terminaong 
the services of Shri Moti Lai Kharwar, Peon-cum- 
waterman, with effect from 10-11-81 is justified ? 
If not, to what relief is tho workman concerned 
entitled ?” 

The case of the workman is that a permanent vacancy 
of peon cum waterman was there at Arya Nagar Branch of 
Hindustan Commercial Bank Limited, Kanpur, which was 
caused due to promotion cum transfer of Sri Santosh Tri- 
pathi working as such from the said branch of the bank. 
Applicant Sri Moti Lai Kharwar was appointed to fill up 
said vacancy living vacant since July 1980. He was appoin¬ 
ted on 4th April, 1981 and was terminated on 9th November 
81 without serving upon him any notice of termination of 
his services or paying notice pay. The workman was also 
not giyen any appointment letter that he was appointed on 
4th April 1981, After termination of the services of the 
Workman one Sri Sheo Kumar was appointed on probation 
on the same vacancy. The applicant has consequently prayed 
for setting aside his illegal termination and for reinstate¬ 
ment with retrospective effect. 

The management In Its Written statement has averred that 
whenever there is permanent vacancy the same is filled up 
from the penal of the approved temporary employees which 
comprises of persons --after adjudging their suitability from 
interview and during the period when a permanent post is 
not filled it is the practice that the branch manager engage 
persons on day to day basis as casual labour. The manage¬ 
ment has admitted that in Arya Nagar Branch of the 
management a permanent vacancy occurred due to transfer 
of Sri Santosh Tripathi peon cum waterman and also a 
permanent hand could be appointed on that post and the 
branch manager of Arya Nagar Branch engaged the services 
of the workman as cafiSal labour on day to tlay basis.' This 
fact was known to .the workman that his appointment was 
purely causal and temporary and that his services would 
stand terminated when a • permanent appointment is • made 
from the penal. At the time of preparation of the penai 
the workman was also interviewed alotigwith other candidates 
but he was not found suitable, hence his name did’nt appear 
in the appeal. That lateron none was appointed as peon 
cum waterman from .the said penal on probation. As fbe 
workman bad not worked for 240 davs he was not entitled 
any wages in lieu of notice or retrenchment compensation. 

Tt is not disputed that the workman worked in. the manage¬ 
ment bank from 4-4-81 to 24-4-81, then 26-6-81 to 19-10-81 
and from 2-11-81 to 9-11-81, Tt is born out from the ad¬ 
mitted documents Annexure B of the management and An- 
nexure I of the workman. The management witness Shri 
Pradeep Sahai has deposed that the workman Sri Moti La! 
was appointed as casual labour. In bi-partite there is no 
category of casual labour rather such casual labour are also 
known as temporary employees vide definition given in para 
29.7 as temporary employee also means a workman who has 
been appointed for a limited period for work is of a essen¬ 
tially of temporary nature. The management witness tried 
to distinguish between appointing a managed engaging the 
man and stated that • word appoint mentioned In para 17 
of his affidavit is wrong. He admitted that when the work¬ 
man was engaged he was not told that he was being engaged 
for a particular period. He further admitted that when he 
engaged Sri Moti Lai Kharwar workman a permanent vacancy 
for peon cum waterman was there which was caused due to 
transfer of one Sri Santosh Tripathi. He further admitted 
that the workman was engaged on that very work and was 
given duty of waterman cum office peon i e. carrying on 
clearing of the records. He further admitted that he was 
essentially employed for this work and did work for the 
span mentioned above. In this way a total work done hv 
him was of 145 davs. He also stated that prior to 4-4-81 
and after 9-11-81 the workman never worked in the tank. 


lie further admitted that the workman was appointed verbally 
and was terminated verbally. As pointed out in para 27 
a casual employee is covcrred as temporary employee and 
even for such employees the management is inquired to given 
an appointment letter and termination letter. Normally the 
management should give the appointment letters even in 
temporary appoiniment wh.ch the management calls causal 
appointment to indicate the period of work, nature of work 
payments for the work done so that these may not remain any 
discrepency regarding the date of termination of casual 
labour. Further in Shaslri's Award para 493. It has been 
recommended that the bank should also maintain a register 
m- which the names of the retrenched and temporary em¬ 
ployees whose work have been found satisfactory should he 
enteicd. It has also been recommended that the bank should 
give first preference to those members of their staff who was 
other than qualified to fill up the vacancy Para 208 of the 
bipartite settlement lays down that a temporaiy workman 
may also be appointed to fill a permanent vacancy provided 
that such appointment shall not exceed from a period of 
three months during which the bank shall make arrange¬ 
ments for filling up vacancies permanently”. In the instant 
case ihe work man was allowed to work as office peon cum 
waterman in the permanent vacancy cieatcd by transfer of 
Santosh Tewari admittedly from 26-6-81 to 19th October, 
1981, which period was more than 3 month and it was 
incumbent in view of the above rule for the management 
to have made permanent appointment for that post as he 
was allowed to work for more than 3 months and the 
management had no grievance regarding the working of the 
workman it will be deemed that having been found fit to 
work and allowed to continue for more than 3 months on 
a permanent post the workman acquired the right for the 
posts to be permanently absorbed and his termination after the 
period of 116 days of work without notice would he illegal 
unjust and iinfa'r labour practice. In view of paragraph 
522 of Shastri’s Award %” 3 month’s notice was required to 
terminate the services of a permanent employees and one 
month’s notice was required for a probationer even treating 
him to be a temporary employee his services could not have 
been terminated without 14 days notice.’’ It may also he 
pointed out that the principal Last come first goof the Shastri’s 
award or sec. 33G and F of the I. D. Act were not complied 
rather one Shri Sheo Kumar was appointed at the place of 
the workman after terminating his services from 9-11-81. 
He further stated that from the date of termination of work¬ 
man and till appointment of Sheo Kumar no one also was 
appointed on that post.' Sri Pradeep Sihal management 
witness stated that the workman never worked after his ter¬ 
mination on 9-11-81, which is fahfltd by the documents 
annexure 1 to 7 which show that the workman worked as 
messenger for bringing the documents of the bank from the 
Head Office during the year 1981, 82. 83 and 84. He also 
admitted that in his branch there are 3 penals for appoint¬ 
ment of workman. 1 he list of the two penals have been 
filed by the workman which is annexure 16 and 17 and 
the thfid has been filed by the management as annexure D. 
He has admitted that the penal which he has filed is a penal 
of sons of the employees of the bank giving preference to 
the sons and daughters of the bank employees is violation 
of article 16 of the Indian Constitution and as have been 
whittled down by Supreme Court in several cases. In 
Kanportba Central Cooperative Bank Limited Vs. Presiding 
Officer Labour Court Jftllandhjtr 1984 Labour and Industrial 
Cases page 974 (Punjab and Haryana High Court) it was 
held “where the services of the workman were terminated 
on their rendering 230 days service with notional breaks when 
the work of the workman was satisfactory and other have 
been recrui'ed in their place. Tt was an instance of unfair 
labour prertice and in this view when the workman were 
hrld entitled to reinstatement then the logical consenucncc 
was that they shonld get their full back wages”. Tn the instant 
case the workman having attained the right to continued 
in permanent vacancy after three months, bis terminat'on was 
illrcnl and the result is that he is entitled to be reinstated 
with full back wages. 

I consepuently hold that the action of tho management 
HCB' in relation to Arya Nagar Branch Kanpur In termina¬ 
tion the services of the workman Sri Motilal Kharwar w.e.f. 
10-11-81 is not justified. The result is that he is entitled to 
be reinstated with full back wages to which he was entitled.

S.O. 1927.—In pursuance of section 17 ol the Industrial 
Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central Government 
hereby publishes the award of Industrial Tribunal, Tamil- 
nadu, as dhoWn in the Annexure in the industrial dispute 
betwen the employers in relation to the management of Bank 
of Cochin Limited, Ernakulam and their workmen, which 
was received by the Central Government on the 8th April, 
1985. 

BEFORE THIRU K. S. GURUMURTHY, B.A., B.L. 

PRESIDING OFFICER. INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL, 
TAMILNADU MADRAS 

(Constituted by the Central Government ] 

Thursday, the 21st day of March, 1985 
Industrial Dispute No. 71 of 1981 

(In the matter of the dispute for adjudication under Section 
10(1) (d) of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 between the 
workman and the Management of Bank of Cochin Limited, 
Ernakulam,) 

BETWEEN 

Shri V. P. Varghese, Valayattu Perambil House, Sara- 
yoor, Post Office Idukki-685620. 

AND 

The Chairman, Bank of Cochin Limited, Administrative 
Officer, Shanmugham Road, Ernakulam. 

REFERENCE : 

Order No. L. 12 o 12/14/83-D.1V(A), Ministry of 
Labour & Rehabilitation. Government of India, 
dated 29-10-1983. 

This dispute coming on for final hearing on Tuesday, the 
26th day -of February, 1985 upon perusing the reference, 
statement of the Management and all other material papers 
on record and upon hearing the arguments of Thiru M. K. 
Kannan, Advocate appearing fo r the Management and the 
workman being absent and this dispute having stood over 
till this day for consideration, this Tribunal made the 
following. 

AWARD 

The Central Govcinment by its Order No L-12012I83-D. 
IV(A). Ministry of Labour and Rehabilitation, dated 
29-10-1983 has referred the following dispute under Section 
10(1)Id) read with Section 7A of the Industrial Disputes 
Act. 1947 for adjudication to this Tribunal. 

(2) The dispute is as follows : 

Whether the action of the management of Hank of 
Cochin Limited, Ernakulam. Cochin in relation to 
their Marayoor Branch in terminating the services 
of Shri V, P. Varghese. Collecting Agent with effect 
from 16-9-19R2 is justified? Tf not, to what iclief 
is the workman concerned entitled? 

13) The parties on receipt of notice appeared before this 
Tribunal, 


(5) The Management in its counter has raised the follow¬ 
ing objections to the claim of the Petitioner; The Petitioner 
Sri V. P. Varghese was engaged as a Daily Collecting Agent 
on contract basis at our Marayoor Blanch with effect from 
20-9-1979. In the agreement executed, it is dearly mention¬ 
ed that (1) the Agent shall not be entitled to churn* or receive 
any salary or allowances or any other benefits that are 
applicable to the employees of the Bank. The Bank re¬ 
serves the right to terminate the same at any time without 
notice if in the opinion of the Bunk the Agent’s performance 
is unsatisfactory or need not be continued. The contract 
between him and the Bank, was terminared on 20-9-1982, 
The termination of the services of the petitioner was in 
terms Of the agreement executed by the Petitioner, The 
action of the Management of Bank of Cochin Limited, Erna- 
kulam is fully justified, The workman concerned is not 
entitled to any relief in this 1 case. The Petition mav be 
dismissed. 

(6) The Management examined one witness M.W. 1 and 
Ex. M-l to M-3 were marked. I heard the learned counsel 
lor the Managmcnt, 

(7) The point for consideration is whether the claim of 
the Petitioner that the action of the Management terminat¬ 
ing his services from 16-9-1982 is not justified, is correct. 

(8) At the outset it should be mentioned that the Peti¬ 
tioner has not indicated the grounds on which he would 
attempt to sustain his claim * because he has not filed any 
claim statement. The M anagement- had contended that the 
Petitioner was ortly a collecting agent finder certain term* of 
contract. The period of agency was one year and the re¬ 
newal wa a on the disetetion of the Bank, Thp Bank had 
always' reserved the authority and power to terminate the 
agency at any time. In support of this slapd of the Manager 
ment, the Management has produced’ the agreement Ex. M-l 
executed bv the Petitioner in favour ■ of the Bank, This 
agieement clearly states that the Petitioner was engaged a» 
an agent for the. purpose of collections from various sub¬ 
scribers under the savings scheme of the Bank. By way of 
remuneration (he agent would be paid commission at tbp 
rate of 2-1/2 per cent on the daily collections made and 
remitted by him into the Bank. The agreement expressly 
mentions that this collecting agent would not be entitled to 
claim or receive any salary or allowance or any other bene¬ 
fits that arc applicable to the regular employees of the Bank. 
Th e agieement contains a clause that the Management has 
got the right to terminate the agency at any time without 
notice. 

(9) The Management has also made it clear that the 
particular savings scheme has been abolished in this parti¬ 
cular branch of the Bank. Therefore the very purpose of 
the agency has ceased to exist. M.W. 1 in his evidence 
would state that this collection deposit scheme was abolished 
by the Bank. The Petitioner was not bound to attend office 
except for remitting the collection made by him. There was 
no attendance register for (his agent at all and no salary 
was paid to him. He was at liberty to attend to other work 
and the only restriction was that be should not act u» 
Collecting Agent for any other Bank. There was no leave 
register for him and he had no provision of casual leave, 
earned leave or any other tvpe of leave which normally the 
regular employees enjoy. He has also made it clear that 
in case the agent fails to make collection, he would not earn 
commission and he will go without remuneration. These 
circumstances emerging from he records and in the evidence 
would conclusively prove that the Management had no con- 
tiol over the professional activities of (his Petitioner, that 
the Management was not paying any salary, that the Peti¬ 
tioner was not under the control of the Management with 
reference to his attendance to the Bank and the Manage¬ 
ment had no control over the earnings of the Petitioner. It 
may so happen that the Petitioner falls ill and he is not in 
a posilion to make collections, Under such contingency, the 
Petilioner will have to deny himself the earnings bv way 
of commission on the collected amount. I have absolutely 
no hesitation to conclude that the Petitioner has not esffi- 



2328 


THE GAZETTE OF 1ND[A . MAY, 4 1935/VAISAJCHA 14, 1907 [Fari II- Sec. 3(a)) 


bllshed his case that he was a regular employee of the 
Bank, This is clearly a case of agency under a specific terms 
of contract and the agency has been terminated and it has 
also come to cease by teason Of the ubolition of the Very 
scheme for which the agency was created. On that view, 

I haVe no hesitation to conclude that the Petitioner has not 
made out a case jkb declaring that the action of the Man¬ 
agement in terminating his services is illegal or unjustified. 

(10) An award is accordingly passed rejecting the claim 
of the Petitioner. There wifi however be no order as to 
costs. 

Dated, this 21st day of March, 1985. 

(Sd/-) K. S. GURUMURTHY, Industrial Tribunal

S.O, 1928.—In pursuance of section 17 of the Industrial 
Di spiff es Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central Government 
hereby publishes the award of the Central Government in¬ 
dustrial Tribunal, Kanpur as shown in the Anne\ure in the 
industrial dispute between the employers in relation to the 
State Bank of India and their workmen, which was received 
by the Central Government on the 12th April, 1985. 

BEFORE SHRl R- B. SRIVASTAVA, PRESIDING 
officer, CENTRAL GOVERNMF.NT INDSTRIAL 
TRIBUNALCUM-LABOUR COURT, KANPUR 

I.D. No. 202/1983 

In the matter of dispute between. 

Shri Ashot Kumar Agarwal, Typist-cum-Clerk C/o 
Shri V. K. Gupta, Dy. General Secretary, State 
Bank of India Staff Association, 2|633 Nambair 
Ajrrt. ...Workman. 

AND 

The Chief Regional Manager, State Bank of India, Lauris 
Hotel, Agrm • ■ Management 

The Central Government, Ministry of Labour vide Order 
No. J>12012/227/82-D.THA) dated 28th June, 1983 has re¬ 
ferred the following dispute for adjudication: 

“Whether the action of the management State Bank 
oj! India in relation to its Aligarh Branch in not 
giving the benefit of his post service for the period 
from 17-9-1973 to 27-9-1974 to Shri Ashok Kumar 


Agarwal Clerk-cum-Typist on his reapopintment in 
the bank, is justified? If not, to wfiat rehet, if 
any, is the Workmen concerned entitled? 

AWARD 

1. The cave of the workman is that he was aropmted as 
Fypist cum Clerk in the management bank ot its Aligarn 
Branch against the tegular permanent vacancy, when hi* 
services were abrhptly terminated on 27th September, 19/4 
without any notice or written orders. That the workman 
had worked for more than 240 days ill one caltender year 
The said termination is illegal as the provision of I.D. Act, 
were not complied wrlh. 'I hereafter, the applicant appeared 
in spe-ial recruitment test in which he was successtul and 
was absorbed perroanenth*in the services of the bank manage¬ 
ment. After the termination of the workman, bank man¬ 
agement appointed a number of fresh candidates in the 
said branch i.e. at Aligarh. The termination of the work¬ 
man being illegal, hence he be treated in service during the 
period from, 27th September, 1974 to 2lst August, 1980 
and pay with all back wages and benefits. 

2. The management has admitted that the workman wa® 
appointed as temporary typist cum Cleik on 17-9-1973 and 
was granted extension from time to tune and last of all 
he was granted extension ol 17 days from 14 to 30th Sep¬ 
tember, 1974. The bank never terminated the services of 
the workman rather the workman himself left the job on 
27th September, 1974 and reported for duty at All India 
Radio, Aligarh on 28th September, 1974. Ihe workman 
left the service without notice or depositing salary and 
allowance for 14 days as required under law. They further 
content that as the workman had completed 240 days service 
in 12 calender months he was allowed to appear in a 
special test and alter qualifying the test he was absorbed, 
in the bank permanently w.c.f 22-7-80. As the bank never 
terminated the services of Shri Ashok Kumar Agarwal 
workman there was no question of following the provisions 
of Shastri’s Awaid or of the Industrial Dispute Act. A* the 
workman has left the services of the bank on his own 
accord thei'c i.4 no question of paying back wages or giving 
him seniority from that date, The management admits that 
the number of new candidates were appointed in between 
28-9-1974 to 21-8-1980, but that has no bearing on the 
claim ol the workman as be has never retrenched. 

3. In support of its contention the management has filed 
the reply ol the Administrative Officer of the All Irfdia 
Radio to the question pose to him. He has intimated that 
the name of the workman was sponsored by the local emp¬ 
loyment exchange and afier typing and interview held on 
14-6-1974 and declaration of the result on that date the 
workman was offered appointment on 20-9-1974 and conse¬ 
quently he join the duties on 28-9-1974. The management 
has also filed letter annexure B of the Administrative officer 
of All India Radio, Aligarh showing that the workman was 
getting total emoluments Rs. 565.20 p. 

4. The management has argued that the W'orkman has 
left the services of the management bank without informa¬ 
tion and notice about his prospective employment with AH 
India Radio and absented himself from the duty w.e.f. 
28-9-74 as on the same day he joined the All India Radio. 
Thus workman Shri Ashok Kumar Agarwal apprehending 
the termination on 30th September, 1974 by effiux of time 
arranged for alternative employment while in the service of 
the bank and voluntarily absented himself from his duties 
and hence the e was no question of terminating his services. 
As the services of the workman weie not terminated by the 
bank management be is not entitled to retrenchment compen¬ 
sation. The management paper annexure B shows that ihe 
workman was relieved from the AH India Radio, Aligarh 
on 21s August, 1980 and he joined the bank’s permanent 
post on 22nd August, 1980 and thus he was not without pay 
cVca a single day. 

5. It is admitted that during the tensure of hfs service 
workman completed 240 davs f.e, in all 371 days. The 
annexure filed by the management show that the workman 
was appointed as temporary workman w.e.f. 14th September, 
1974 for 14 days ending on 30th September, 1974 and it 
was clearly mentioned that in case the workman wish to 
leave the service before that day he should give 14 days 
notice in writing to this effect,

6 . The whole case of the workman boils down to deter¬ 

mination o fthe fact whether the management terminated the 
services of the workman or he himself left the service of 
the "bank management. Provision of Sec. 25F will not 
attracted unless it is established that the workman was re¬ 
trenched, The definition of the retrenchment under Section 
2 (cc) means that the termination by employer of the 

service of a workman tor anv reason whatsoever, and thus 

employ that there should be positive act of the terminated 
employee, 

7, The document ext. 3 shows that the temporary employ¬ 

ment of the workman was continue till 30th September, 1974. 
There doesn't appear any reason why the maagemeut would 
terminate his service abruptly 3 days before the upOpinted 
date of termination i.e. 30th September, 1974. On the other 
hand the documents annexure A and B testify that the work¬ 
man reported for duty on 28-9-74 at AH India Radio, Ali¬ 
garh on the basis of the apointment oxered on 20-9-1974 

given on the basis of lest and inteiview held on 14-6-1974 

Hnd result declared the same day. This shows that it was 
under cotempltttion ol the workman that the services of the 
bank wa9 temporary and was extended from time to time, 
he may better to try his luck else where and consequently 
entered in All India Radio services on 28-9-74 when he 
co-uld have still worked for three days more i.e. uplo 30th 
September, 1974, The facts of the case might have been 
otherwise had the workman continued in bank service till 
30-9-1974 and his services going to end by efflux to time 
without compliance of the provision of section 25-F of the 
I.D. Act. 

8 , Under the circumstances, 1 am not e a dined to believe 
the Verssions of the workman and believe the evidence of 
the management, Thus the action of the management tank 
of the State Bank Of India in relation to its Aligarh branch 
in not giving the benefits of hi s past services for the period 
17-9-73 to 27-9-74 to Shri Ashok Kunur Agarwal Clerk 
cum-Typist is justified and he is not entitled to any relief. 

9. I, therefore, give my Award accordingly. 

Let 6 copies of this award be sent to the Government 
for publicatfbn.

S.O. 1929.—In pursuance ol section 17 of the Industrial 
Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central Government 
hereby publishes the award of the Central Government indus¬ 
trial Tribunal Kanpur, as shown in the Annexure in the 
industrial dispute between the employers in relation to the 
State Bank of India and their workmen, which was received 
by the Central Government on the ]2th April, 19S5. 

BEFORE SHRI R. B. SRIVaSTAVA, PRESIDING 
OFFICER, CENTRAL GOV F,RNMENT INDUSTRIAL 
TRIBUNAL-CUM-LAEOLR COURT, KANPUR 

LD. NO. 207i83 

Tn the matter of dispute between : 

Shri Annorudha Kumar Sharma, Temporary Typist/ 
Clerk, C/o Shri V. K. Gupta, 2/363, Namnair. 
Atrra ■ Workman, 

AND 

The Chief Regional Manner, State Bank of India, Tau- 
ries Hotel, M. G, Road, Agra. Management 

Shri V. K. Gupta representative—for the workman, 


Shri S. S, Sharma, renrsci.tative—for the bank Manage¬ 
ment. 

AWARD 

The Central Government Ministry of Labour vide its order 
No. L-12012/220/82-D. : II(A) dated 28th January 1983. 
referred the following dispute joi adjudication : 

“Whether the action ol the mangemeni of the State 
Bank of India, Agia, under the contiol of the Chief 
Regional Manager, Agra in terminating the services 
of Shri Annurudra Kumar Sharma, tempotaty typist 
clerk with effect from 22-2-74 is justified? If not, 
to what relief is the workman concern entitled?” 

It is common ground that the workman Shri Annurudr# 
Kumar Sharma was appointed on 24tJi May, 1973, but it 
is disputed that ho was appointed against regular and per¬ 
manent vacancies. According to the management he was 
appointed purely on tompotary basis and an appointment 
Icier for one month was issued to him. The photocopy 
of that letter has been filed by the management as Annexure 
A which shows that the appointment was given only foi 
one month as temporary. The management bunk has ad¬ 
mitted that the penod for appointment was extended and 
thus during the span 24-5-73 to 22-2-74, when his services 
were terminated, he had completed 269 days of work in a 
span of yea r counting from the date of termination. It is 
further admitted that no notice was given as the same was 
not required and that no ictrenchment compensation was 
paid to the workman. The mangement has however, staled 
that to give workman the benefit of his temporary services 
he was called for test and interview held specifically for his 
category of employee, The mangement however, raised a 
preliminary objection that the claim of the workman is very 
old and is not entcrtainablc, in view of the fact that the 
workman has been given employment subsequently bv the 
management. Further that the workman is stopped from 
claiming benefits in view ol subsequent acceptance of the 
employment and also undeitaking given by him. 

The workman has culled the termination illegal as hi* 
services were terminated without notice, notice pay or re¬ 
trenchment compensation or without following the prov isions 
of last come first go laid down under Sec. 25(g) of the l.D, 
Act. 

The workman had appeared in the examination conducted 
by the bank on 3rd October, 1982 without prejudiced to his 
rights and claim, then pending before the Asstt. Labour 
Commissioner (Central), Agra. Despite objection before 
the test the bank management under pressure and threnU 
got singed a declaration that in case he was apointed he 
will not claim past benefits, consequently after passing the 
test and interview, the workman was appointed on 4th April. 
1983, but he was not given the benefits of his past services 
and back wages and continuitv of the service. The work¬ 
man has prayed that he be deemed in regular appointment 
of the bank from the date Of his initial apopintment and 
should be paid full back wages and be treated in con¬ 
tinuous service. 

The mangement hank has moved a miscellaneous appli¬ 
cation offering concessions to tha workman. It is averred 
that the mangement has reconsidered the matter in its 
entirety and has come to an agreement with the AH India 
State Bank of India Staff Federation at Central Office I evel. 
The workman is also a member of the association which is 
affiliated to the federation. According to the understanding 
reached between the workman and the management, the 
management offers to pay all arrears of salary and allow¬ 
ances to the Workman w.e f. 16-1-76 i.e. the date on which 
the judgment in Sundermone.v’s case was announced bv the 
Supreme Court subject to fulfilment of conditions. 

Further this concession was not to mean that the work¬ 
man will enjov the benefit of continuity of service for the 
period he has not worked with the bank for the purposes of 
seniority. 

My attention was also drawn during the course of argu¬ 
ment to the agreement between the Stale Bank of India and 
All India State Bank of Tndia Staff Federation, dated 22nd 
August, 1977, wherein it was agreed : 



2350 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 4, 1985/VAiSAKHA 14, 1907 


[Part 11 — Sec. 3 (ii)J 


“That each Of the concerned temporary employees will 
be paid bach wages together with such increments as would 
have been admissible to him as temporary employee in the 
hank service for the period upto the date of leinstatcirjent, 
from the date of judgment namely 16-1-76 if the date ol 
last tetminatiott of service was earlier than 16-1-76 and ficm 
the date of last termination it it is later than 16-1-76”. 

Further clause 4 of the agreement lays down as follows : 

“The concerned tcmpoiary employee will not be eligible 
for any special benefits including seniority in service as 
flowing front the reinstatement in service other than what 
ire noimally provided for m the terms and conditions of 
the service of temporary employee ia the bank”. And clause 
V tuns as follows :— 

“All dispute imsed by any affiliate of the Federation or 
any individual employee pr any body else in regards to the 
benefits of back wages seniority in service, increments or 
other benefits following from reinstatement have been settled 
by virtue of this agreement and that the parties to such dis¬ 
pute shall report this agreement for being recorded by any 
authority like an Industrial Tribunal Court, conciliation 
officer or any other authority before whom dispute ccy be 
pending and all such disputes shall no longer subsist and 
be deemed to have beep withdrawn.” 

In the said agreement there is no agreed period during 
which the settlement remained to opeiation and in the ab¬ 
sence of such, agreement outlived its utility after expiry of 
six months under Section , 19(2) of the I.D.Act. 

Further the government in its wisdon referred the dispute 
for, adjudication ignoring any such agreement. T astly the 
All India Federation of beneficiary employees hank, infudi- 
cigry capacity vis-a-vis workman of other hank have no 
right- to jespardise the interest of the workman or bind them 
against the legal benefits accruing and to that extent the 
agrement have not any interest of the workman and again** 
law of the land would be illegal and not binding. 

Sec, 2fcfo)' of ■ T.D. Acl definieq “retrenchment which 
includes -termination for any reason whats-o-ever unless falls 
in ,any of the accepted categories.” 

Sec, 25B(2) defines the condition of service. It is not' 
disputed that the workman was terminated [retrenched w.e.f. 
22-2-74 when he had put in more than 240 days of work 
during the span of one year counting hackwords from 
22-2-74. Tt is further not disputed that no retrenchment 
dues, nofice or notice pav wan given to him as required 
under sec. 25(f) of the l.D. Act, hence in view of the law 
laid clown in State Bank of India vs. M. Sundermonev 1676 
SC. Cases (L&S) page 132- the retrenchment would be 
illegal ad-initio and the workman will Ire entitled to be put 
back where he left i e. reinstated with all full back wages, 

Tn Mohan Lai Vs. Bharat Electronic Limited, 1981 S.C, 
Cases (T &S) page 478, it was held as follows : 

“We held that the termination of the service of the 
appellant was ab-initio void and in operative and 
the declaration is made that he continued to he 
in service with all consequential benefits namely 
,, bagk wages in full and other benefits, if any.*' 

In B. N. Guola Vs. State of West Bengal. Civil Revision 
No. '1826/W/76 dated 21st \ugust, 1978. T n this case it 
was observed : 

“It is provided that the petitioner be reinstated forth¬ 
with and will he paid full back whrcs from the 
date of apopintmcnt/reinstatement till the reinstate¬ 
ment minus in sum paid to the petitioner under (he 
proper receipt in the meantime”. 

Tn view of the above discussion and law laid down I vive 
mv Award that the action of the management of S'ate Bank 
of Tndia Agra under the control of the Chief Regional 
Manager. Agra in terminating the services of Sr! / tnnnrud r n 
Kumar Sh.-urna temporary ivnist/clerk w.c f, 22-2-74. is not 
justified. Shii Annurudrn Kumar Shnrmn will be deemed 
to Be in continuous service thereafter and will’ be entitled 


to full back wages from 22-2-74 till the date of his re- 
apopintment i.e. 4-4-83. He -will be entitled to the continuity 
of service and other past benefits. 

J, therefore, give my Awaid accordingly. 

Let six copies of this award be sent to the Government for 
publication. 

R. B. SRIVASTAVA, Presiding Officer

Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central Government 
hereby publishes the award of the Central Government 
industrial Tribunal Kanpur, as shown in the annexure in 
the industrial dispute between the employers in relation to 
the Slate Bank ot India and their workmen, which was 
reccvcd by the Central Government on the 12th April, 
1985.

CER CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL TRIBU-

AWARD 

The Central Government Ministry of Labour vide order 
No. L-12012/2/81-U7I' (A) dated 25th September, 1981 
referred the following dispute for adjudication ; 

“Whether the action of the management of State Bank 
of India Varanasi in termination the services of 
Shri Raja Ram Sub-staff with effect from 14-6-80 
is justified 7 If not, to what relief is the workman 
concerned entitled 7" 

The fase of the applicant workman is that ho was appoin¬ 
ted as tnassenger by the bank management on 15-3-80. That 
suddenly his services were terminated on 13-6-80; that neither 
appointment letler n,or. terminalion letter was issued to him. 
Do further ascert that his appo ; ntment was against a perma¬ 
nent post yet his services were terminated suddely without 
notice or notice pay. Tt is further contended that 
the management appoints workman, for 85 days or 91 days 
and then terminates his services and appoints another man in 
place of the terminated workman ahd all this is done with 
every view of depriving the workplan with benefit of conti¬ 
nuity of service,.which is a unfair labour practice and victi¬ 
misation. The workman has consequently prayed that his 
termination be held as unjust and unfair, and he should be 
ordered for reinstatement w.jth. Full back wages and bene¬ 
fits. 

The case, of the management is that-the Raia Ram was 
appointed as temporary massenger for a period of 91 days 
from 15-3-80 to - .13-6-80. against purely temporary require¬ 
ments of the management. That on 13-6-80 the appoint-

ment of Rujaiam expired by afflux of time. The workman 
accepted hs dues till then „without any protest. It is how¬ 
ever admitted that at the time when the services of the work¬ 
man automatically expired by afflux of time there was one 
permanent massenger working at the branch who was senior 
to him. It is further admitted that no letter of termination 
or appointment was issued in writing. The management 
however averred that the workman may claim the wages 
in lieu if any, payable to him. In the end the management 
has averred that after termination from 13-6-80 the workman 
is gainful employee and his earning not less than what was 
getting from the bank. The management bank consequently 
prayed that the termination be held valid. 

In the rejoinder it is averred that appointment of tempo¬ 
rary hands is permissible in banks but 14 days notice before 
termination of service is mandatory. Further no temporary 
appointment can exceed 90 days. It is further averred lhat 
in addition to a permanent hand in the hank the appoint¬ 
ment was as additional hand in the hank and prior to the 
workman one Sri Sooraj was appointed as temporary hand 
for 83 days and after 83 days terminated the workman and 
subsequent to the termination of Rajaram anolher workman 
as appointed thus violating secs 25G and 25H of the I. D. 
ct anj rule 77 and 78 of the I. D. Central (Rules!. 

The management hank filed affidavit of one Sri Nav. 1 
Kishore an officer in the management bank. In cross exami¬ 
nation he admitted that the workman was appointed as 
casual workman without appointment lettfcr and was also 
terminated without any letter of termination. He further 
slated that one Ram Kumar a casual workman joined at 
Ayodhya Branch from 14-6-80 for 88 days. It may be 
mentioned that it was at Ayodhya branch where the work¬ 
man worked for 91 days and regarding which the management 
itself has filed the certificate paper No. 2 of list dated 
19-9-83. This shows that soon after termination i.e. on 13th 
June 80 Sri Ram Kumar was appointed on the very next 
dnv i e. from 14th June, 80. The workman in hfs affidavit 
averred that he was appointed as massenger on 15-3-80 but 
no appointment letter was issued to' him nor duration o f 
aopointment was intimated and suddenly his services were 
terminated on 13-6-80 and after termination Of his services 
another person Sri Ram Kumar was enaaged n his rihee 
from 14-6-80 and that before his appointment one Sooraj 
was working in his place as massenger and his services 
was also terminated. In cross, examination he has admitted 
that there was another massenger in the bank When the 
workman was working there and the npmc of that massenger 
was Jamuna who was working there from before h's appoint¬ 
ment. He has denied that on the day he wad appointed 
he was told that his services will come to an end on a 
particular date and after his removal Sri Ram Kumar would 
be appointed as massenger. 

From the averments of the nttrties afid the statements of 
the witnesses it emerges that the workman'was employed in 
the management bank from 15-3-80 to 17-6-80 without 
issuing the appointment letter or giving him the termination 
letter. According to the manat"'ment witness the abnoint- 
ment of the workman was casual and after termination oi 
the services of the workman one Ram Kumar lottied the 
hank as casual workman at the' same branch on 14-6-80. 
If the work of casual nature "Was there the’ WOrkmhn should 
have been allowed to continue and not terminated on 13-6-8(1 
and another man appointed on ’the’ next day. The appoint¬ 
ment of Sri Ram Kumar shows ’ that the nature of work 
done bv the workman Rajaram-continued even after hia 
termination. The workman has averred that he was appoin¬ 
ted as temporary massenger. Tn para' 4 of ’ the rejoinder 
the workman has averred that in' addition to the permanent 
hand he was appointed as additional nfid Prior to him one 
Soorai was appointed for a period Of 83 dovs. Fven casual 
employees arc included in the definition of temporary em¬ 
ployees given In para 2(3.7' of the bipartite settlement and 
they come in that category of ertmlove'e and was appointed 
for limited period for work' nthich'Is of a essential temporary 
nature As no appointment IrttCr W"s ’Issued b can not be 
said that the nnnointrrtent' of the'applicant workman w"s for 
a limited eeriod and h : s termination came automatically Tv 
efflux of time mentioned In th’e appointment letter Thus 
he would he ratted' a cnsitffl'of 'if temporary emolos ee of 
this nature, During the erbss of The fvOrVm’an fhr 'mpieseu- 
tatlve for the management NSink ' argtled" that railing the 


workman as temporary employee would not be proper as 
a matter of fact he was additional hand as he himself ad¬ 
mitted in para 4 of the lejomder. In the absence of appoint¬ 
ment letter it can not be said that he was temporary em¬ 
ployee as a additional workman in connection with the 
temporary increase in work of a permanent nature. F' pm 
if it could conceaded that Rajaram was employed as addi¬ 
tional hand in connection with tempoiary increase of work 
in permanent nature, continued even after 13-6-80 and 
it was on that account that from 14-6-80 one Ram Kumar 
was appointed for that work foi that temporary increase of 
work of permanent nature was continuing, it was unfair 
labour practcie on Ihe part of the management not to have 
allowed the workman to continued and employed another 
person after terminating his services particularly when the 
branch manager was satisfied with h s working in view of 
the certificate issued on 7th Tuly 80 and filed by Ihe manage¬ 
ment. It is not the case of any party that the workman 
was appointed in a tempoiary vacancy caused by absence of 
the permanent peon. If the permanent nature of work of 
massenger was there a temporary workman could be appoin¬ 
ted to fill the vacancy for a period not exceeding 3 months. 
In view of para 208 of the Shastri’s award in which it is in¬ 
cumbent of the bank to make arrangement for filling up 
the vacancy permanently. The workman Raia Ram was 
allowed <o work exceeding three months i.e. from 1,5-3-80 
to 13-6-80 for more than 91 days in that permanent nature 
of job. 

In para 493 of the Shastri’s award it is recommended that 
the bank should give first preference to those members of 
their retrenched staff who arc otherwise qualified to fill up 
the vacancy. Thus the workman should have been continued 
after 91 days and Ram Kumar should not have been em¬ 
ployed at his place. 

Tn nnra 495 of the said Shastri’s award it is laid down 
“In all other cases the probationer after expiry of the period 
of--six months should be deemed to have been confirmed 
unless their services are dispensed wth from or before the 
expiry of period of probation. We further direct that on 
a candidates appointment as temporary employee a proba¬ 
tioner or a permanent member of the staff, the bank, shall 
give him a written, order specifying the kind of appointment 
and the nay and allowances to which would he entitled and 
that such n written order shall he given on the appointment 
of a part lime employee also”. 

As observed earlier no appointment letter was given to 
the workman specify ’he ,pay or period. Had the workman 
being allowed tp continue after 13-6-80 and Ram Kumar 
would not have been appointed at his place he might have 
completed 6 months of appointment as the branch manager 
was satisfied with his working. 

Section 425 0 f the Shastri’s award lais down that even 
in ease of temporary emnlovec 14 davs not'ce of the termi¬ 
nation shall he given which was not done in the instant 
case. 

Tn view of the above T am not inclined to agree with the 
arguments of the management representative that the services 
of the workman terminated hv efflux of time and when the 
workman know about his appo'ntmcnt ay -hr did not raised 
anv objection for appointment or hue and Civ. In the 
absence of or evidence T am not inclined to agree that the 
anpoinlment of the workman was due to temnornrv Increase 
in work of permanent nature and was a temporary require¬ 
ments of the branch It can not be- inferred that he was 
not required >n fill the nost of nermanenl nature of work 
when one person was alreadv working in (ho branch. Thus 
in thw rase in the uhsence of anonintlliept leper, termina¬ 
tion letter end appointing another man for the sSme' post 
' held Ih'it it was unfair labour practie- of the management 
hank to havc terminated the services of ifie workman wEen 
he likely to acquire status of a permanent employee after 
working for 6 months on that post, when he had already 
worked frir three months and for another 3 months another 
person Was appointed. 

T, therefore, give my award that the action of the manage¬ 
ment of State Bank of Tnd'a Vqrangsi fn terminating the 
services of Sri Rajaram Sub-staff w.e.f, 14-6-80 is not justi¬ 
fied, The effect 'Is that the workman shall be reinstated in

AWARD 

The Central Government by its order No. L-1701I/8/81 / 
D-IV(A)/date^l 17-9-1982 has referred the following dispute 
for adjudication under Section 10(1 )(d) ie«d with Section 7A 
of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 to this Tribunal. 

(2) The dispute is as follows ; 

Whether the action of the management of Life Insurance 
Coi-poration of India in relation to the Southern 
Zona] Office, Madras, in changing the hours of work 
of 18 technical employees of the Engineering De¬ 
partment (Class-Til and Class-IV) to 8.30 to 12,30 
hours and 13.30 to 17.30 hours (with lunch break 
from 12.30 to 13.30 hours) as per the notice of 
change of service condition dated 8-5-1981 is justi¬ 
fied 7 If not, to what relief aie the workmen con¬ 
cerned entitled ? 

(3) The parties on receipt of notice issued hy this Tribu¬ 
nal appeared and the Union filed the claim statement raising 
the following grounds in support of its case. Ip the Zonal 
Office Engineering Deportment of the Respondent-Coipora- 
tion, the following are the staff strength : 

Class HI ...6 

Class IV (Maintenance)

Their working hours normally is only from 8.00 A.M. to 
12.00 Noon and 1.00 P.M, to 5.00 p.M. with a lunch break 
of one hour in between. The Respondent Corporation for 
appointment made before 1960, treated the Engineering De¬ 
partment employees same as that of other employees work¬ 
ing in the Corporation for whom the working hours are only 
from 10.00 A.M. to 5.00 P.M. with 45 rump/es lunch break- 
The daily working nours for the employees were only 6-1 /4 
and for Saturday it was 3J hours only. In the Engineering 
Department such of those employees who were appointed 
before 1960 are still enjoying the right of their working hours 
fixed at 6-1/4 hours per day only. The employees in Engineer¬ 
ing Department have been directed to come at 8,00 A.M. 
for the reason of coordination and work allotment. The 
employees did not mind the existing working hours because 
they were also allowed to leiwe at 5.00 P.M. along with 
other employees. This had greatly enabled them to partici¬ 
pate both in the Union activitcs and also to avail transport 
facilities to reach their homes in time. The employees were 
satisfied with the existing working hours even though the 
parity of working hours was a long felt demand of the em¬ 
ployees as well as of 'the Petitioner-Uhion. These employees 
working in the Engineering Department whenever they work 
beyond the normal working hours were getting overtime. 
The employees who do not have any working hours prescri¬ 
bed in their appointment orders were no! asked to do the 
8 hours of work. Only “those employees who are appointed 
subsequent to I960 and for whom working hours were speci¬ 
fied in heir appointment orders were made to work 8 hours.

with one hour lunch briak in between. They also fried to

The Petitioner-Unfon took up the matter and wrote to the

Tun- 1981 and the daily working hours wfie from 8 30 A.M.

hours of work. The Management has indicated in Ex, M7

(16; The learned counsel appear ng for the Union relied 
on £x. W-14 to sustain the plea that the worker must ac¬ 
count for the tools evciy day at the office and therefore 
he cannot carry tools to the residence, I am afraid that the 
language in Ex, M-14 does not support the stand taken by 
counsel for the Union Under Ex. W-14 the Management 
has indicated that if the workman has taken the tools to 
his house and is not taking the tools available at the time 
of inspection, it will be irregular. Ex. W-14 meiely states 
that the tools supplied to the worker for use in the office 
must be available at the office and taking -those tools home 
when they are Intended to be used at the office will be 
irregular. This does not in any way annihilate the evi¬ 
dence of M.W l who a Class-I Officer that ‘the employees 
who are supplied tools for out-door work can take the 
tools home if the work remained unfinished. His evidence 
that they need not report again on the next day and they 
can go to the workspot straight to do work also remains 
unaffected. HTs evidence makes it abundantly clear that 
•the convenience of the workman and the nature of the work 
to bo done by them have been taken into consideration by 
the Management. 

(17) The inse dixit of M.W. 1 that the proposed change in 
the timings will cause incowenlcnce to the workmen and 
will affect their participaion in the union activities is uncon¬ 
vincing and is not supported by any specified data. When 
the Union expects the member-employees to narticipate in the 
union activities, is left to guess work. Now the employees if 
they are to leave the workspot after 5.30 P.M. will be 
affected by traffic unavailability is again nef clearly indicated 
Therefore I have no hesitation to conclude by the 
proposed change in the timings by fixing the hours 
of work between 8.30 A.M, and 12.30 P.M. and from 1 30 
P M. to 5.30 with lunch break from 12.30 P.M to 1 30 
P.M. the Management has not increased the hours of work 


and the change in the timings w.ll not in any way cause 
prejudice to the employees, 

(18) On that view, an award is passed rejecting the claim 
of the Union, Fhcie will however bo no order as to costs. 

Dated, this 26th day of March, 1985. 

K. S. CURMURTHY, Presiding Officer.

ihe oruer dated 8th December, 1979 of the Bank should bo

inter se seniority as Accountant since 1966 or 1969 with fit¬

aiso prayed that the Bank should be directed to cred.t his 
sick leave fiom 1975. 

2. The Bank has filed its written statement at Ex. 3. 
The Bank has therein taken a prel minary conlention regard¬ 
ing the maintainability of the present complaint under Sec. 
13A of the Act. Without prejudice to this prelim nary con¬ 
tention, the Bank has denied the various allegations made 
in the complaint. According to the Bank, the complainant 
was called tor interview by the Bank and was advised after 
the interview that he has been selected for promotion 
in accordance with the rules and regulations in force in 
that behalf and are applicable to all such promotecs in the 
employment of the Bank. However the complainant imposed 
certain conditions of his own on the Bank which the Bank 
was not willing to accept for the simple reason that ihe 
terms and conditions imposed by the compla nant were 
not only absurd and unreasonable but the same were not 
in conformity with the rules and regulations which are in 
force in the establishment of the Bank. The Bank has then 
contended that the complainant is in fact not interested n 
any promotion but he is bent upon harassing the Bank on 
one pretext or the other, The compla inttnt is an office-bearer 
of ‘he All India Punjab National Bank Employees’ Asso¬ 
ciation, Delhi and is also the General Secretary of the Pun¬ 
jab Nat onal Bank Workers' Organisation, Bombay and as 
such he wants to continue to remain as a workman. If he 
accepts the promotion, he will not be able to invoke the pro¬ 
visions of the Industrial Disputes Act. 1947. The Bank has 
then contended that it offered promotion to the comnlainant 
which he refused to accept unconditionally and therefore 
the Bank had rightly presumed that the complainant was not

conditions of the complainant have neither been altered nor 
cnanged in any manner whatsoever and therefore the com¬ 
plaint men oy n.m is raise and oaseiess. in the end, the name 
nas stated in its written statement, Lx-i, that the Bank is 
suit ready and waling to oner promotion to me complainant 
stricuy in according with its rules and regulations in force 
provided me complainant accepts such piomouon Without 
any precondition and any icservation. The complainant has 
hied a rejoinder, Lx. 4, m reply to the Bank’s written stat¬ 
ement, Lx.x, in wmch he has denied me statements and 
allegations made in the Bank’s wiitteu statement. He has 
empntuneiicany aenied therein that he, at any time, had 
imposed any unjust or atottrary or ausurd condition of nis
