had never retused to accept the promotion as contenued by 
the Bant. 

3. The complainant has sought production of a number of 
documents by me Bank and it appears that after discussions 
it was decided that a joint inspection report of such docu¬ 
ments snouid oe submitted to the tribunal. Accordingly, such 
a joint report was submitted, Ex,7|l. Out of these documents, 
several important documents were then produced by the Bank 
(Lx, 9 to 9|14). It then appears that in support of his case 
tne complainant had examined himselt (Lx. 8) wh le the Bank 
had examined its Assistant Manager at Bombay (Lx. 14). 

4. Shri K. V. Shah, the learned advocate appearing for the 
Bank firstly argued fit respect of the preliminary contention 
that the present complaint is not maintainable under Sec.33A 
of the Act. Shri Shah contended that looking to the nature 
Of the references that were pending before me Tribunal at 
the time when this complaint was filed, the complainant is 
not a workman concerned m the disputes covered by those 
references and as such no complaint under Sec,33A would 
He. In support of this contention, Shri Shah has relied on the 
decisions reported in 1964 11 L.L.J. 143 and 1977, Late IC 1135 
As agamst this, Shri K.N.Malhoira, the complainant contended 
that ne was a protected workman and also a workman con¬ 
cerned in the disputes pending adjudication at the material 
t me. Shri Malhotra further argued that a similar point was 
decided by the Learned Industrial Tribunal. Shri R. C. lsrani 
in Complaint (TIC) No. 1 of 1976 before him in which it 
was helj that the complainant therein was a workman con¬ 
cerned in the relevant reference before him. Shri Malhotra 
aigued that the facts of the present case are similar to those 
in Complaint (ITC) No. 1 of 1976 referred to above. Shti 
Malhotra further argued that the cause of the workman in 
each of the pending references, though it constituted an in¬ 
dividual grievance, was espoused by the body of the workmen 
and as such each workman was interested m the result. Shri 
Malhotra also argued that outcome of even an individual 
dispute which was deemed to be an industrial dispute under 
Section 2A would also affect all the workmen of the em¬ 
ployer. It appears that a similar question arose before the 
Hon’ble Supreme Court in Tata Iron and Steel Co. Ltd. Vs. 
Singh (D.R.) reported in 1965 II, L.L.J., 122 where.n Their 
Lordships of the Supreme Court observed as under:— 

‘‘Logically, it would be necessary to make a finding on 
the preliminary point raised by the appellant before 
the merits are considered, because if the appellant is 
right n contending that the respondent is not a 
“workman concerned with such disputes” within the 
meaning of S. 33(2), the application would be un¬ 
necessary and there would be no jurisdiction in the 
tribunal either to accord or to refuse approval to the 
action proposed to be taken by the appellant against 
the respondent.” 

It would, therefore, be proper to make a finding on the 
preliminary point raised by the Bank before the merits 
are considered. 

4. In order to decide this point, we shall have first to look 
to the relevant provisions of the Act. F rst of all, we shall 
have to refer to Sec. 33A under which this complaint has 
been filed, which is as under :— 

“33 A. Where an employer contravenes the provisions of 
section 33 during the pendency of proceedings be¬ 


fore a Labour Court, Tribunal or National Tribu¬ 
nal, any employee aggrieved oy such contravention 
may make a complaint in wrjtmg, m the prescribed 
manner, to such Labour Court, Tribunal or National’ 
Tribunal and on receipt of such complaint that Labour 
Court, Tribunal, or National Tribunal shall adjudi¬ 
cate upon the complaint as if i* were a dispute 
referred to or pending before it, in accordance with 
the provisions of this Act and shall submit its 
award to the appropriate Government and the pro¬ 
visions of this Act shall apply accordingly”. 

So, a complaint would he if an employer contravenes the 
provisions of Sec. 33 during the pendency of proceedings 
before a Tribunal. Therefore, it would have to be seen if in 
the piescnt case the employer i.e, the Bank has contravened 
the provisions of Sec. 33 during the pendency of the procee¬ 
dings before the Tribunal. However, before we consider the 
same, it would be convenient to dispose of one point. It is 
contended by the complaint 'that he is a ‘protected workman 
within the meaning of sub-section (3) of Sec. 33 of the Act. 
This is not denied in the written statement of the Bank nor 
is it challenged in the cross-examination of the complainant. 
It will therefore have to be taken for granted that the com¬ 
plainant is a ‘protected workman'. Therefore, the relevant 
portion of See, 33 would be sub-section (3) which reads as 
under:—• 

“(3) notwithstanding anything contained in sub-section 
(2), no employer shall, during the pendency of such 
proceeding in respect of an industrial dispute, take 
any action against any protected workman concerned 
in such dispute— 

(a) by altering, to the prejudice of such protected 
workman, the conditions of service applicable to 
him immediately bel'oic the commencement of 
such proceeding; or 

save with the express permission in writing of the autho¬ 
rity before which the proceeding is pending. 

It is clear that the above sub-section applies only when(i) 
such proceeding in respect of an mdustiul dispute is pending; 
(ii J the workman concerned is a protected workman, and 
(hi) the workman is concerned in such dispute. If these cond-i 
tions are fulfilled, the sub-section prohibits the employer from 
taking any action against the protected workman concerned 
in such dispute by altering to the prejudice of such protected 
workman, tbe conditions of service applicable to him imme¬ 
diately before the commencement of such proceedings. It has, 
therefore, to be seen whether these conditions are fulfilled in 
the present case, in order to made the complaint maintainable. 
So far as tbe first condition about pendency of proceeding is 
concerned, there is no dispute that at the time when this com¬ 
plaint was made. Reference (ITC) Nos. 4 and 6 of 1975 and 
2 and 5 of '976 were pending before the Industrial Tribunal 
from whom the present complaint is transferred to this* 
Tribunal, As for the second condition, as observed above, the 
complainant was a protected workman at the relevant time. 
In regard to the third condition, the contention of the Bank 
is that the complainant was not a "workman concerned” in 
any of the disputes pending adjudication before the Tribunal- 
As against that th e argument of the complaint is that though 
these disputes relate to individual workman in each case, the 
question involved in those disputes would always be Important 
for all the workmen employed by the Bank. As such, accord¬ 
ing to the complainant, he was a workman concerned in those 
disputes. There are a number of decisions on this point but 
the decisions which are binding on this Tribunal are the 
decisions of the Honblc Supreme Court In ; (1) New India 
Motors (Pvt.) Ltd. Vs. Mommin (K,T ) reported in 1960-1, 
L.L.J. 551 and (2) Digwadih Colliery Vs. Rami l Singh, repor¬ 
ted in I964-II, L.L.J. 143, It is true, as artrued by Shri 
Maibotra, the complainant, that the Learned Tribunal Shri 
R. C. Tsrani. relying on the decision In 1960-1 L.T.J. 551 has, 
in his award in Complaint (ITC) No 1 of 1976, held that 
the complainant in that case was a workman concerned, but 
it seems the subsequent decision of the Hon’ble Supreme 
Court, which made the position abundantly clear, was decl- 
brought to the no*fee of the Learned Tribimal. Tn that deci¬ 
sion viz. in 1964-IT I .L.J. 143, their Lordship^ of the Sup¬ 
reme Court observed: 



[Part II- She. 3(ii )} 


233S THfi GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 4, 1985/VAISAKHA 14, 1907 


‘‘Unless the natuie ofi the pending dispute was ascertained 
and considered, it could not be said that the con¬ 
cerned workman was a workman concerned in the 
pending dispute simply on the ground that thore \sas 
some lefeicuce pending'. 

Applying the above idling of the Hon’ble Sup/eme Court 
in Knagandra Prasad Patra V. D. T, M. S., T. K Komput and 
anoiher, 1976 Cab. 1. C. 1260, tile Oinsj High Court obscued 
as utiner :— 

“The clause forbids the employer to niter to the prejudice 
of the workman lire conditions of his service only 
if the woikman is the workman “concerned m Mien 
dispute'. It does not debar me employer nom tattl¬ 
ing the conditions of service or taking any such 
step in regard to any other workman, Tno word 
“Concerned , accoiding to th; Wcbstei’s LOiveisai 
Unabridged Dictionary means “interested; engaged; 
having a connection with'’ Accoiding to Shorter 
Oxford English Dictionary it means “mtciested. 
involved”, in order to substantiate the ch,im ot 
contravention under Sec. 33(1)(a) the workman 
theietore has to snow that he was conecinej w.ttt 
the-pending dispute in any of the munncis env suged 
by the expression. The but den of nrovmg this will 
naturally be on the workman. 

Tne learned counsel foi the petitioner urged that since 
me pending dispute haa been taxon up t>v me 
Union ot which me pemoncr was also a mernoei, 
me dispute was a dispuie between tne employe 
and an me members ol the Union and as such 
attracted tne piovisions or section JjUJ (a). vve 
are unaole to accept the submission, I he mere tact 
that the petitoncr wns a member of the Union 
(assuming this to be so) will not make him a work¬ 
man "concerned" in the dispute witmn tne mean.ng 
of Section 33(1) (a). It is the dispute that tne 
workman has to be concerned with and not cmy 
with the parties to the dispute, it is ascertain- 
ing the nature of tho pending dispute that tne 
Court can reach the conclusion whetner the work¬ 
man is "concerned” with it or not”. 

Although Sec.33(1) (a) is referred in the above decision, 
tne reasoning equally applies to Sec. .-3(3) (3) became the 
term "workman concerned" is the same in both the cases. 1 
am in respectful agreement wrth the above rulings ot the 
Urissa High Court and 1 also feel that the burden of prov¬ 
ing that he was a “workman concerned” in the pending dis¬ 
pute rests on the workman and the mere tact that tne 
workman was a member of the union (or even an ofiice- 
bearer of the union) will not make him a ''workman con¬ 
cerned" in the pending dispute 

5. The combined effect of the above decisions is that there 
must be some common features in the nature of the dispute 
in the two cases which should serve as a connecting link 
thereby rendering the workman iu the latter case also a 
"workman concerned” m the dispute in the earlier case, and 
the burden of proving this link will be on the workman, 
Therefore; it will have to be seen whether in the present 
case the complainant has proved that there were some com¬ 
mon features in the nature of the disputes in the four re¬ 
ferences which were pending at the relevant time on the 
one hand and the dispute rn the present complaint on the 
other. Now, so far as this point is concerned, the com¬ 
plainant in. his complaint. Ex. 1, has made an averment that 
he is also the Vice President of the All India Punjab National 
Bank Employees Association, Delhi whose industrial dis¬ 
putes under reference Nos: 4ij_5, 6[75, 2|76 and 5|76 under 
Sec. 2Ck) o fthe Act. are pending hearing before the Tribu¬ 
nal and the complainant is therefore concerned in these dis¬ 
putes. In his written arguments. Ex. 19, the complainant has 
stated as under so far as this point is concerned 

“Fact that the complainant is concerned and connected 
in the main industrial disputes pending before, This 
Hon’ble Tribunal which were laised by the All 
India P, N. Bank Employees’ Association of which 
the complainant i s the Vice-President (besides and 
apart from his being protected workman) stated in 
Para 1 of Ex. 1 (complaint) and the same has not 
been denied by Bank in Ex. 3 viz. written state¬ 
ment. Besides, a similar objection was raised hv 


Bank in the Complaint (ICC' No. 1 of 76 vi. the 
case ot fate Shii N. M. Pauchai, who was executive 
committee member of Ail India P.M.B. Ernpl. 
Assn, and also, like tne complainant. General Sec¬ 
retary ot Gujarat Mate Unit of the said Association. 
His complaint was also similar and identical' and 
was under the present pending references as in tne 
present case. This Tribunal have an Award published 
m Gazette of India Part II, Sec. 3(ii) on Pages 
3440-46. This Tribunal while over i tiling the said 
objection discussed at length this issue m paras 9 
to 19 and ou the basis ot 1960-1 L.L.J. 551 (SC) 
(New India Motors (P) Ltd. V[S M'oris (K. T.J in 
para 16 and A.l-R. 1959 Pun. 360 para 17 Shri 
iPanchal was bel'd to be concerned and connected 
in rara 18 and also on the basis of phraseology 
of order of references he was held to be concerned 
workman in para 19” 

More or less tlic same thing was reiterated in his oral 
uiguincms, so iji as tins aspect is concerned, ,-cs iai us me 
decision or tne Learned tribunal inn K. G. isiani in tne 
case ol Min ,n. m. panciral is concerned, I nave aircuuy ic- 
lcned to me same aboVe. Following itie decision or tne 
Hon ole Supreme Court in 1964-iI l.L.J. lay and oluer 
decision cited above, 1 respectfully differ with the conclusion 
arrived at by toe Learned Tribunal Shn ft. C. isrant (to 
wnom leierence is mad© as "fins Tribunal’ in the written 
arguments ot complainant reproduced above, tne present 
compiamt having been later transferred to me) in that case. 
Now, trom the above it is apparent that the complainant Uas 
not at all shown as to how he was concerned m the pending 
disputes. 

6. Even then, let us look to the nature of the disputes 
cove!mg the lour references viz., Kef. (ITC) Nos. 4|7o, 6|/5, 
2)76 and 5)76 which were pending. So lar as Ret. (11C) 
Nos. 2|76 and 6|76 are concerned, it appears lrom the Con¬ 
solidated award of the Learned Tribunal in these two cases, 
whicn is on lecord, that Uiey related to two temporary godown 
keepcis. Their services were terminated on various dates 
and after a break eacti tunc they were given fresh appoint¬ 
ments. Ihe contention of the two wokmen in those leie- 
rences was that those terminations were illegal and without 
justification and therefore the Bank should be directed to 
conlirm them on the expiry of their one year’s service and 
they should also be paid wages for tbe periods during winch 
they were illegally kept out of employment, etc. In legaid to 
Ret. (ITC) Nos. 4|75 and 6|75, while detailed information 
in regard lo them lias nut been brough. on record, I have 
seen the relative orders of the Government of India refer¬ 
ring the disputes for adjudication to the Industrial' Ttibunal. 
The schedules annexed to these orders mention as under :■—- 

In case of Ref. (ITC) No. 4|75 

"Whether the All India Punjab National Bank Em¬ 
ployees Association, Delhi is justified in demanding 
reinstatement and confirmation of Njri B. C, Shah, 
temporary Godown Keeper of Punjab National 
Bank, Dabhoi branch of the said Bank 7 If so, 
from what dale and to what relief is the said work¬ 
man entitled 7” 

In case of Ref. (ITC) No. 6|75 

"Whether the management of the Punjab National Bank 
Bombay, is justified in terminating the services of 
Shri R. iP. Parikb, temporary Godown Keeper at 
Kapadvani with effect from 14th August, 19737 If 
not, to what relief is the said workman entitled 7” 

Refinding the above terms of these two references, one 
would be led to believe that the dispute in these two refer¬ 
ences is more or less the same as in {he other two references, 
mentioned above. They also concern temporary godown 
keepers and their termination of services. Now considering 
the nature of the disputes in all these four references (on 
the basis of the information that is before me), I do not 
think there is auy common feature or conecting link between 
ihe disputes in the above references and the dispute in the 
present complaint. When that is so, the complainant would 
not he a “Workman concerned” in those disputes, and if he 
is not a “workman concerned" one of the necessary condi¬ 
tions in sub-section (3) is not fulfilled. As such, there would 
nfft be any breach of the provisions of Sec. 33 and no com- 



[■fiPT II-SFT 3 (ii)] 


urc tww mf 4 , 1 sss/t^im 1 4 , 1907 


2339 


plaint under Sec. 33A would lie. This tribunal would, there 
fore, have no jurisdiction to consider the present complaint. 
The present complaint would, therefore, have to he rejected 
as not maintainable before this Tribunal, and it is so rejected. 

In this view of the matter, it is not neccssaiy 'to consider 
the merits of the complaint and the complaint stands dis¬ 
posed of. No order as to costs.

AWARD 

The Central Government by its Older No, L-43015I1)/

(2) The dispute is as follows :

fb) to the ex-employees of M/s. Hopkin and Williams

what should he the basis for fixing the relative

in order to give them preference in re-employment 
as per section 25H of the Industrial Disputes Act '! 

(3) On receipt of notice issued by this Tribunal, parties 
appeared. 

(4) The Petitioner—Union filed the claim statement raising 
the following grounds to sustain the claim of the workmen : 
The Travancore Minerals Limited. Chavara was a Public 
Sector Undertaking jointly owned by the Government of

India and the Government of Kerala. It has three plants, 
two at Chavara and one at Manavalakurichi in Tamil Nadu. 
M/s. Hopkin & Williams Ltd,, was a Minerals Company in 
Chavara owned by a private Company. On the 1st ot Oct¬ 
ober, 1960 the said Company was closed down and all work¬ 
ers were given closure compensation under Sec non 25irFF 
of the Industrial Disputes Act. Some amounts were due from 
the Company to the Government of Kerala. The Company 
was entrusted to a custodian Sri George, a Chief Engineer 
of the Company. In December, 1962 one of the Branches 
of Travancorc Minerals Limited in Chavara was shut down 
and“the other one also followed suit in February, 1963. The 
workmen employed in these two Units were also retrenched 
under Section 25 of the Industrial Disputes Act. In respect 
of employees of Plant No. 2, there was some dispute 
regarding the retrenchment compensation and the Manage¬ 
ment in answer contended that it was not a case of retrench¬ 
ment but of closure. The District Court, Quilon by its judge¬ 
ment dated 6th February, 1968 declared that it was not a 
closure but a ‘retrenchment’. By that time, the Indian Rare 
Earths Limited, had acquired interest in the two companies 
and the Indian Rare Earths Limited was the 4th Respondent 
in the case before the District Court, and thereby is bound 
by the decision rendered bv the District Court, Quilon, The 
Indian Rare Earths Limited, purchased the assets of Travan- 
core Minerals Limited, as a going concern in January, 1965 
and continued to function the Company with Head Office at 
Quilon. The work was carried on in the Plan in Manava- 
lakuruchi. In May, 1968 one of the Unions which was 
earlier representing some of the workmen of the former

to allow only those who have not attained the age of 45 
years among the ‘retrenched workmen’ to be re-employed 
in the Indian Rare Earths Limited. All ex-employees were 
entitled to re-employment in terms of the judgement of the 
District Court, Quilon. Based on the aforesaid alleged agree 
ment the ex-employees who were declared as ‘retrenched 
workmen* were continuously denied employment by the man¬ 
agement of the Indian Rare Earths Limited. The Manage¬ 
ment of the Indian Rare Earths Limited started employing 
totally new employees who have absolutely no right. 

(5) The Janatha General Woikers Union, took up the

workmen who have been domed employment from 1971 
onwards should be not only provided with work ■ but also 
should be compensated. Ihe workmen should be declared to 
be eligible to all benefits with arrears due to them with 
effect from 1-1-1971.

Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central Government 
hereby publishes the award of the Industrial Tribunal, Jaipur 
a» shown in the Annexure in the industrial dispute between 
the employers in relation to the Divisional Engineer, Tele¬ 
graph, Bikaner and their workmen, which was received by 
the Central Government on the 17th April, 1985. 

CENTRAL INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL. RAJASTHAN, 
JAIPUR 

Case No. C.I.T. 51/84 

REFERENCE : 

Under Secretary, Government of India, Ministry of 
Uabour, New Delhi Order No. L-40O12(4)/83-D. 
TI(B) dated 7 6-84. 

Tn the matter of an Industrial Dispute. 

BETWEEN 

Shri Roopchand, S/o Shri Gangaram, through Shri Bharat 
Singh Sengar, Advocate, Near Daga School, Bikaner. 

AND 

Divisional Engineer Telegraph, Biknner. 

Sub Divisional Engineer, Telegraph, Bikaner Board Office, 
Bikaner. 

PRESENT : 

Smt. Mohini Kapur, R.H.J.S. 

For the Applicant.—-Shri Bharat Singh, Advocate. 

For the Opposite Party.—None. 

Date of Award : 5-2-85 

AWARD 

The Central Government ha c referred the following dispute 
for adjudication under Section 10(1) of the Industrial Dis¬ 
putes Act. 

“Whether the action of ?&T management in relation to 
their Divisional Engineer, Telegraph, Bikaner, is 
justified in terminating the services of Shri Roop- 
charid Choudhary, Casual workman with effect fiom 
30-4-827 Jf not, to what relief is Shri Roopchand 
Choudhary entitled?’’ 

2. The workman in pursuance of notice appeared Iicfoie 
me and submitted his claim while the opposite party i.e. 
Divisional Engineer, Telegraph, Bikaner has not put in ap¬ 
pearance in spite of notices and proceedings against him arc 
ex parte. 

3. According to the statement of claim the workman Shri 
Roop Chandra, was employed under the Divisional Engineer 
Telegraph, Bikaner in the Hnmnmngarh Sub Division in 
March, 1973 at the rate of Rs. 9.50 per day. He continued 
to work as a casual labour and on 2T1-80, he was posted 
as a dally wage worker against the post of chowkidar, which 
is a class fourth post. He continued on this post In (he 
office of Sub Divisional Officer, Telephones, Bikaner. On 
24-4-82, he was selected for regular service by order of the 


Divisional Engineer Telephones, Bikaner dated 13-4-82. 
However his services were orally terminated on 30-4-82 with¬ 
out assigning any reason, or giving him anv notice, or 
pay in [ieu of notice, or any other compensation. He had 
worked for a number of years and foi more than 240 days 
in each year and was entitled to protection under section 
25 F and 25 G of the industrial Disputes Act. As the pro¬ 
visions of the law has not been complied with and there are 
posts with the opposite party on which persons junior to the 
workman Shri Roop Chandra are working, he has claimed 
reinstatement with full back wages. 

4. The version of the opposite party is not before me and 
I have to decide the case on the affidavit of .Shri Roop 
Chandra and documents produced by him The failure report 
in conciliation proceedings gives the vers'On of the manage¬ 
ment which is that the workman left the service in April, 
1981 and after that he worked with the S.D.O. (Tj, Bikaner 
for about six days in May urd June and thereafter left the 
service. The workman has pioduced F\ r , 2, which is a letter 
dated 13-4-82, by which the Divisional Engineer Telephone, 
Bikaner informed the workman hat he had been selected 
for appointment to the cadre of icgi.Uu ma/door. Ex. 3, is a 
letter by which he was appointed as a chowkidar on daily 
wages w.e.f. 8-1-80. Ex. 4, shows that [he workman Sbri 
Roop Chandra was working with S.D.O. (J), Bikaner till 
15-7-81 and thereafter was chowkidar with Divisional En¬ 
gineer Telephone, Bikaner. Copies from the muster roll have 
been produced to show that he worked from 1973 to l')8L 
It is evident that he had been working as a mazdoor for a 
considerable length of time. He had been in continued ser¬ 
vice for more than one vear It cannot be believed that 
after being selected fo r regular appointment he himself left 
the job. The removal of such workman attiacts the provisions 
of Section 25 F of the Industrial Disputes Act. The termina¬ 
tion of service amounts to retrenchment as it does not fall 
within the exceptions to Section 2(oo> of the \ct. The 
workman has stated on oath that his '•civiees were terminate I 
and there is no evidence to contradict him so as to disbelieve 
him. His service have been terminated which amounts (o 
retrenchment made without following the provisions of Sec¬ 
tion 25 F and 25 G of the Industrial Disputes Act and in 
therefore invalid. The W'orkmar is entitled to be leimtntcd 
in service with full back wages. 

5. An award i» passed that the termination of the seivice 
of Shri Roop Chandra by the Divisional Engineer Te’epbnne, 
Bikaner is illegal and not unified He is entitled tn be 
reinstated in service with full hack wages. 

6 . The award i* sent to the Central Govt, for publication. 

SiMT. MOHTNI KABUR, lodge.

S.O. 1940.—In pursuance of section 17 Of the Industrial 
Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central Government 
hereby publieshes the award of the C cntral Government 
Industrial Tribunal, New Delhi as shown in the Annexure, 
in the industrial disputes between the einployefs in relation 
to the management of Indian Airlines New Delhi and their 
workmen, which was received by the Central Government 
on the 17th April, 1985. 

BEFORE SHRI O. P. SING LA, PRESSIDINO OFFICE, 
GENERAL GOVT. INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL, New 
DELHI 

I. D. No. 126|80 
In the matter of disputes between 

Shri P. S, Kbctrapal, Pilot/Captain r/o R-12A, Hauz 
Khas, New Delhi. 

Versus 

The Managing Director, Indian Airlines, 113, Gurd- 
wara Rakabganj Road, New Delhi-110001. 
PRESENT : 

Shri S. K. Bhasin and Asok Bhasin for the Manage¬ 
ment. 

None for the workmen. 

AWARD 

Central Government, Ministry of Labour on 17-11-80 
ride Order No. L-11012(3)'80-D, 11(B) made reference of 
the following dispute to this Tribunal for adjudication: 

“Whether the action of the management of Indian Air¬ 
lines Corporation. New Delhi, in not providing 
suitable ground job in the Corporation to Shri 
P. S. Khetrapal, Fx-CaptainlPilot in the Corpora¬ 
tion, with effect from 6-11-78 and paying him an¬ 
nuity without taking hts option on becoming medi¬ 


cally unfit for flying duties is justified 7 If not, to 
what relief is the workman concerned entitled ?” 

2. Shri P. S. Khetrapal served as a Pilot with Indian Air¬ 
lines for a number of years and lose to the status of a Cap¬ 
tain there. On 12-1-73 he took off on a flight from Nagpur 
to Delhi. He diverted the fight to Agra. His case is that he 
had confirmed it with the weather information fed by Delhi 
Air Traffic Control Authority but when he landed there the 
weather position is said to have not been the same as con¬ 
veyed to him. There was said to have been somo quarrel 
with the Air Force Staff posted at Agra. 

3. His complaints is that he was declaied medically perma¬ 
nently unfit as suffering Irom Giddiness on account of de- 
partmantal officers being cntmical to lii nl and he was re¬ 
lived from his flying duties w.e.f. 1-12-1975. His point is 
that the Corporation did not give him ground job as per 
service rules and practice prevailing with the Corporation 
but merely offered him annuity which was accepted by him 
under protest upto October, 79 when even the voluntary 
annuity was stopped by the Management. He stated that 
the action of the Management is not providing him ground 
job was totally unjustified. He claimed Rs. 5,07,3761- as 
under :— 


ENTITLEMENT AND CLAIM 

1. 2-9-1974 to 31-3-1975 72,000.00 

2. 1-4-1975 to 31-3-1976 1,34,188.00 

3. 1-4-1976 to 31-3-1977 1,34,188.00 

4. l-4-1977to 28-8-1978 87,000.00 

5. Medical and other expenses incurred 

in India and abioad. 60,000.00 

6. Superannuation and Retiring benefits 20,000.00 


1974-1978 


5.07,376.00 Appr. 


and he also claimed, that the Management should give him 
a suitable ground job and also pay him annuity by way of 
interim relief. 

4. The Management of Indian Airlines contested the 
claims and asserted that the reference was not maintainable 
and Ihe subject matter of the reference did not amount to 
an “Industrial Dispute” within the meaning of section 2-A 
of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 and th c dispute has 
not been espoused by Union of workmen. 

5. Tt was not a case ol termination of employment. There 
was said to be well-defined service conditions for employment, 

6. On facts it was pleaded that employee concerned was in¬ 
volved in an accident at Agra and after holding domestic 
enquiry in respect of his mis conduct in terms of Standing 
Orders applicable to him he was given serious warning in 
August, 1974 for his- lapse in navigation. When he filed an 
appeal and was given personal hearing the Appellate Autho¬ 
rity did not interfere with the orders already passed. 

7. An Air Craft can only be operated by persons who 
arc licensed in the manner prescribed by thc Competent 
Authority and a Pilot can fly the aircraft so long us he holds 
a valid current licence issued by IXICa and such licence is 
current only when the Pilot i3 medically fit. The claimant was 
declared permanently medically unfit w.e.f. 1-12-75 upon his 
rrcdical assessment by the Medical Board, New Delhi, bona fide 
and was so informed and could not be given Pilot duty. The 
employeo concerned as per service conditions applied for 
benefits under Annuity Scheme in Junf, 1976, His licence 
was renewed on 30-11-77 and 13-2-78 on conditions of re¬ 
view. He was declared unfit in thc Medical Examination held 
rn 10-8-78. He sought review of the decisions by the 
D.G.C.A. where it was held that his hearing acuity was below 
standard. In the Special Medical Examination held on 8-5-79 
he was assessed permanently unfit for flying because of sub¬ 
standard hearing. 

8. In the circumstances aforesaid he could not be given 
flying duties. As regards the dispute regarding alternative 
ground job or payment of annuity, it was said to be a dis¬ 
pute not covered by section 2-A of the T.D. Act, 47 Tt was 
never espoused by any Union, The employee demanded from 
the Management restoration of flying duties alone. Thc ground 
job was demanded as interim relief. The order of reference 
was said to be invalid for that reason as well. 

9. It is stated that the employee concerned approached 
the Management and requested that annuity be paid to him 



[ 1 TPT II—tJW j (H)] 


KTCt W TT«|qa : 4, 1385/ falTO 14, 1807 


2347 


and he entered into an agreement with the Corporation on 
11-3-82 for payment of annuity in terms of clause 8 of Reha¬ 
bilitation agreement for payment of annuity to Pilots declared 
medically unlit and for that reason the reference had be¬ 
come redundent. 

10. The matter has been tried. Ihe workman sent an 
application for amendment of the reference in exercise of 
powers of this Tribunal by referring to Order 6 Rule 17 and 
Section 151 OPC stating that he had been paid annuity be¬ 
cause it was due to the workman after the workman ceased 
to be an employee of the Manage me in but he was not en¬ 
titled to job of Pilot or Ground Job which wt: not given to 
him. 

11. The application was rejected because amendment of 
the reference is not a power vested in this Tribunal and the 
person presenting it did not have any power of attorney 
from Khctrapal and the workman was declared ex parte. 

12. In view of the fact that the workman has opted for 
annuity during reference pendency on 11-3-82 and annuity has 
been paid to him and he has also got the Provident Fund 
and Gratuity amounts drawn from the Indian Aiilines, the 
reference has become redundant. Even otheiwisc the reference 
is not covered by Section 2-A of the l.D, Act, 47 because 
it does not relate to termination of service and rather refers 
to not providing him suitable ground job and giving annuity 
without taking option. 

13. In any case the workman has taken his annuity by 
himself opting for the same the reference has become redun¬ 
dant and the workman Is not entitled to any relief. 

The Award is made accordingly. 

Further it is ordered that the requisite number of copies 
of this Award may be forwarded to the Central Government 
for necessary action at their end 

APRIL. 10, 1985.

S.O. 1942.—Whreas the Contra] Government having 
been satisfied that the public interest so required had, in 
pursuance of the provision of sub-clause (vi) of clause in) 
of section 2 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1917 14 of 
1947), declared bv the Notification of the Government of 
Tndia in the Ministry of Labour and Rehabilitation, Deptt, 
of Labour S. O. No. 3159 dated the 21st September,, 1984 
the service in any Oil field to be a public utility service for 
the purposes of th e said Act, for a period of six months, 
from the 19th November, 1984, 

And whcieas, the Central Government is of opinion that 
public interest requires the extension of the said period by a 
furthei period of six months; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the poweis confericd by 
the pros iso to sub-clausc (vi) of clause (a) of section 2 of 
the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 ol 1947), the Central 
Government heieby declarer the said industry to be a public 
utility service for the purpose of the said Act, for a further 
period of six months from the 10th May, 1983.

S.O. 1943.—In pursuance of section 17 of the Industrial 
Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central Government 
fiereby publishes the award of the industrial Tribunal, Madras 
as shown in the Annexure, in the industrial d snue between 
the employers in relation to the management of Tndia Cement 
Limited. Sankari West and their workmen, which was received 
by the Central Government on the 15th April, 1985. 

BEFORE 7H1RU K. S. GURUMURTHY, B.A., B.L., 
PRESIDING OFFICER, INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL, TAMIL- 
NADU, MADRAS 

(Constituted by the Cential Government) 

Friday, the 29th day of March, 1985 
Industrial Dispute No, 16 of 1982 

(In the matter of the dispute for adjudication under Section 
10111(d) of the Industrial Disputes Act. 1947 between the 
workman and the Management of India Cement Limited, 
Sankari West-637303), 

BETWEEN 

The workman represented by 

The General Secretary, 

India Cements Enmloyees Un on, 

Sankari West-637303. 

AND 

The General Manage;, 

India Cements Limited, 

Sankari West-637303. 

REFERENCE ; 

Ordei No. L-29012(7)/R1-D.II1 (B), Ministry of Labour, 
Government of India, New Delhi. 

‘I his dispute coming on for final hearing ori Wednesday, 
the 13th day of February, 1985 upon perusmg the reference, 
claim and counler statements and all other material papers 
on record and upon hearing the arguments of Thiruvalargal 
Row and Reddy. Advocates appearing for the workman and 
of Thiru S. Jayaraman, Advocate appearing for the Manage¬ 
ment and this dispute having stood over till ! his day for 
consideialion th s Tribunal made the following, 

AWARD 

The Central Government by its Ordei No. L-29012(7J/ 
81-D.IJl (B), Ministry of Labour, dated 27-2-1982 has 
referred the following dispute for adjudication to this 
Tribunal. 

2. The dispute is as follows : 

Whether the action of th e management of Messrs India 
Cements Limited in teiminating the services of 
bhri S. Subramani, T, No. 135, e\-E,irtb Toadcr of 
their Karuma Parathanur Lime Stone Quariy with 
effect from 11-8-1978 is justified. If not, to what 
lel.ei is the concerned workman entitled ? 

3. On icccipt of notice i'sucd by this Tribunal, the parlies 
have ungeared. The Union filed the claim statement raising 
the following grounds in supporl of the claim of ihe work¬ 
man : Shri P. Subramani was working as an Earth I oadcr 
at the Kariimpurathanur D'me Stone Quariy, maintained by 
the Respondent-Management. On 11-8-1978, the Respon- 



2349 


[*TTff II—*5^? 3 (li)] 


'4R5m i, 1985/^ffn? 19, 1907 


(lent-Management gave a letter to the said workman that 
the (Juany Medical Officer has found him unfit lor work 
us he was suffeiing from Leprosy. Another letter was given 
on 8-11-1978 slating that if the worker is aggrieved he can 
appeal before the District Medical Officer Salem. The said 
\voiker appeared before the District Medical Officer, who 
in turn sent him to the District Leprosy Offlcei, The Peti¬ 
tioner understands that the Officer gave a negative report 
i oganhnu the disease. He never received any commun ca¬ 
tion fioni the District Modical Officer, lie lecoived an 
order daleu 19/22-12-1978 from the Respondent-Manage¬ 
ment, lerminaling him from service on the ground that the Dis¬ 
trict Med cal Officer has found him unfit to worn tn the 
qmirifes. The Management did not furnish him with the 
report of the District Medical Officer, Die worker went 
and got himselt examined by an Assistant Surgeon of the 
Oovernment Headquarters Hospital, Salenu who is also a 
Sk n Specialist. The said Doctor (Dr. K. T. Rajagopal) 
gave u ceilificatc stating that the disease is a non-infections 
one and thid he can do his regular duties and that he is fit 

for any type of work. He was also certified by the Civil 

Suigeon of the Depaitmcnt of Dermatology and Leprosy, 

tttached (o the Oovernment Stanley Hospital on 5-7-1979. 

The Department certified that Ihc leprosy is a non infectious 
one and that he is lit to continue to perform his duties. 
After he was certified by Two Specialist he wrote to the 
Management, Petitioner Union wrote to the Management 
by their letter dated 29-10-1979. The Management was not 
agreeable to reinstate the worker. The management by their 
letter dated 8-8-1980 submitted that they are not in a posi¬ 
tion to reconsider their earlier stand. 

4. The termination of the services of Shri P Subramanl 
is wholly unjust and illegal. The Respondent-Management 
also cannot ignore the medical evidence produced by the 
concerned worker from twm Spec alisls as well as by the 
same District Medical Officer, Salem. The worker has put 
in moie than 9 years of service, It is really unfair on the 

part of the Management to deprive him of his livelihood. 

The worker is medically fit to do any type of work. The 
<1 sease is not in any way infectious one. In terminating 
his services the Respondent-Management have not followed 
the procedures prescribed under the Standing Orders. The 
said tci ruination of th c workman is a retrenchment within 
the meaning of Section 2(oo) of the Industrial Disputes 
Act, They have not followed the mandatory conditions 
precedents, pi escribed under Section 25-F of the Act. The 
said termination is void abintio. This Honourable Tribunal 
may be pleased to pass an Award directing the icinstale- 

ment of thc worker Shri P. Subramani In service with back 

wages, continuity of service and all other nttaudant benefits, 
award costs and render justice 

5. The Management resisted the Union’s claim by raising 
thc following grounds in the counter statement : There was 
a settlement between the Management and thc Contractors, 
As per Clause 3 of the settlement dated 8-6-1978. 395 work- 
.men then working under the sub-conti actors were to be 
absorbed on the permanent rolls Of the Respondent-Manage¬ 
ment, subject to their being found medically fit by the 
Company’s Medical Officer to be free from the following 
defects, namely : (al Physical infirmity, (b) Cancer, <ci 
Tuberculosis, ( d) Leprosy and (e) Heart ailment and subject 
further to right of appeal to the District Med cal Officer, 
Salem by the concerned worker. Sri Subiamuni was dec¬ 
lared unfil by the Med cal Officer of the Respondent, on 
ttie ground that he was suffering from Leprosy. Vi Subra¬ 
mani as also 43 others who were declared unfit on various 
grounds uuc icferreil to the District Medical Officer, Salem, 
for medical examination by him. in order to enable (he 
weakens to exercise their r ght of appeal. The understanding 
was 'hut the decision of the District Medical Officer shall be 
final and binding on both the workmen and the Management. 
Medical Officer had declared 8 as unfit ,one of whom was 
Sii P. Si-ubramani. Tn view of this medical disqualification, 
ot the ftcspondem-Management, 

0, Once the worker has exeicised his right of appeal and 
the Appellant Authority (District Medical Officer, Salem) has 
declared the worker unfit, thc question cannot be reopened. 
Sri Suhlamnni has exercised his right of appeal befoie thc 
District Medical Officci, Salem, as slated above and his 
subsequent appearances before the various Medical Authori* 
lies and getting fitness certificates after a considerable lapse 


of time, have therefore no relevance. There is no provision 
in Ihe Certified Standing Ordeis for the same. The Respon¬ 
dent-Management is therefore not bound by these certificates. 

T he copy of the medical certificate of the District Medical 
Officer, Salem has been received by h m on 27-3-1980. There 
is no provision either in the settlement dated 8-6-1978 or 
the minute' of discussions dated 20-10-1978 for calling tor 
the expert Opinion in case of varying medical opinions. 
The Respondent denies that the Petitioner’s attention was 
not brought to the fact that he can appear before the Civil 
Assistant Surgeon. The allegation that the D strict Medical 
Officer has slated in his letter dated 25-5-1980 that the skin 
mean for A.F.B, was found negative, does not improve 
the case of the Petitioner. The Respondent does not admit 
that the District Med cal Officer founds him fit to join 
duty The termination was done in accordance with thc 
Standing Orders. Thc question of retrenchment does not 
arise at all in the present case. This is a simple discharge 
from scrv.ee on medical grounds in accordance with the 
provisions under the Certified Standing Orders. It does not 
attract the provisions under Section 25(F) of the Act. Re¬ 
ference to Clause 17A of the Certified Standing Orders has 
no iclevancc. The claim of the Petitioner Union is not 
maintainable in law and on facts. The Honourable Tribunal 
may be pleased to dismiss the same with costs. 

7. MW-1 and WWs-1 nnd 2 were examined. Exs. M-l 
to M-l3 and W-l to W-16 weie marked. I heard the learned 
counsel for the Petitioner-Union and the learned counsel 
for the Respondent-Management, 

8. The point for consideration is whether the order of 
the Management terminating the services of the workman 
Sri S. Subramani with effect frojn 11-8-1978 is justified. 

9. The order of dismissal passed by the Management 
dated 19-12-1978 is Ex. W-4. Th’s order refers to the 
ground on which the services of the workman were termi¬ 
nated, Thc ground is that the District Medical Officer, 
Salem uficr examining the workman Sri Subramani had 
certified that he is unfit to do the work in the Mines. There- 
fote the disqualification on medical grounds i® the ground 
for termination of the services of the workman. It would 
necessarily compel the Tribunal to consider whether thc 
workman was found medically unfit to do the work. At 
first thc doctor who exam'ncd the workman Sri Subramani 
and gave ceitiflcate that he is medically unfit to do the 
work is the Company doctor who has been examined as 
MW-1 before this Tribunal. MW-1 has stated that he 
examine^ nearly 395 workmen and this Sri Subramani is 
one among those 395 workmen. He has given the report 
Ex. M-2 stut ng that Sri Subramani is suffering from leprosy 
and therefore he is unfil to do the work. The very exami¬ 
nation of the workman by the doctor was In puisuance of 
tne terms of thc settlement between the Management and Ihe 
Unions. That settlement is marked as Ex, M-l. Ex. M-l in 
clause (3) states that these workers who were working under 
the unlicensed contractors would be absorbed on thc perma¬ 
nent rolls of the Company with effect from 1-4-1978 with 
contlnuitv of service, however subject to the workers- being 
found by the Company’s Medical Officer to be free fiom the 
enumerated diseases and one of the enumerated diseases is 
leprosy. The very settlement contemplated a right of appeal 
against the Company doctor’s opion to the D’stricf Medical 
Officer Salem by the concerned worker. Therefore thc 
certificate ; ssued by the Company’s doctor MW-1 even under 
the terms of the settlement would not be final. In fact 
MW-1 admitted that of the 44 persons certified to be unfit 
by him, 36 were cerlifled to be fit by the District Medical 
Officer, Salem. 

10. The Management has given the letter dated 8-11-1978 
to this workman Sri Subramani asking him to exercise hi® 
right of appeal in terms of Ex. M-l settlement dated 8-6-1978 
by reporting before the District Medical Officer for exami¬ 
nation and this letter of the Management is Fx, M-3. Exs. 
M-4 nnd M-5 clearly prove that this workman Sri Subramani 
bad appeared before the Assistant District Medical Officer, 
Salem for examination along with other persons, Under 
Ex. M-4 the Assistant District Medical Officer. Salem has 
said that this Sri Subramani is medically unfit. The ground 
on which this Assislant jDstrict Medical Officer had certified 
this workman unfit has to be found in the certificate Et. 
M-5 dated 27-11-1978. Tn Ex. M-5. the Assistant District 
'Med'cnl Officer has stated that this Subramani is suffering

from Tuberculoid Hensen disease and therefore he is not 
fit. But there is one important aspect that should be noted 
in the certificate Ex, M-5, namely, that this District Medical

this Sn Subramani has any communicable disease or that

it clear tnat everything was normal with this workman 
excepting the leprosy reflected in a small patch on his left 
Buttock region. If the disease is not communicable it is 
rather d ffieult to accept that by reason of such non-commum- 
cablc disease the workman will become unlit for the job. 
It is also necessary to indicate that both MW-1 and the 
District Medical Officer who has given Ex. M-5 certificate 
are mere Renerul physician^ with no expertise knowldcge 
with refeience to the leprosy. MW-1 could not deny that 
th s District Medical Officer who gave Ex. M-5 certificate 
is not a skin specialist. He admitted that he is not a skm

might give d fferent opinion after examining this patient Sri 
Subramani, MW-1 also admitted that the leprosy found 
in this workman Subramani is not contagious and it is 
cuiablc, 

11 . It is not understanable how in spite of these admis¬ 
sions MW-1 should persist in saying that the disease will 
hamper the physical labour to be done by the workman. 
As against this evidence adduced by the Management the 
workman has examined the doctor, namely, District Leprosy 
Officer, Salem as WW-1. This WW-1 the District Lepiosy 
Officer, Salem was working as the Leprosy Officer in the 
District. He stated that he examined this worker Sri Subra¬ 
mani and gave the certificate Ex. W-l. At that time he 
was the Medical Officer in charge of the post of District 
Medical Officer, Salem. To that extent this certificate Ex. 
W-l, the doctor made it clear that this Subramani is not 
Medical Officer, Salem and a specialist in leprosy. In Ex. 
W-L the doctor madt it clear that this Subramani is not 
having any skin disease and ho is fit for duty. In evidence, 
WW 1 asserted that when he examined this patient Sri Subra¬ 
mani he had no skin disease and he certified him to he fit 
for the job. He asseited that he is physically fit to do any 
work. He also made it clear that the disease noted in Ex. 
M-5 is not infectious. In fact he made it clear that the 
disease ment’oned in Ex. M-5 depending on the insistence 
power in the body of the patient and depending upon the 
age of the individual may get automatically cured. He could 
of the patient Sr* Subramani. Therefore the evidence of 
find any contraction in the fingers or other limbs 
WW-1 who is an expert aud the most competent man to 
deal with the sk'n disease completely supports the case 
of the workman that he is fit physically to do the job 
in the Mints of the Respondent-Management. The work¬ 
man examined himself as WW-2 and has made it clear 
that he got certificates from specialists and those certificates 
are Exs. W-14 and W-16. He refused the suggestion that 
he created these certificates for the purpose of this case. 
It is impossible to accept the theory that experts dealing 
with leprosy cases and exparte attached to hospitals would 
stoop lo the level of giving false certificates. 

12 Coming fo the certificates themselves, they are marked 
as Ex. W-9, W-14 and W-16. W-14 is dated 21-12-1978, 
It should be borne in mind that the letter of the Management 
fo the workman to appear before the District Medical Officer 
in exercise of his r ight of appeal provided by the terms of 
the settlement F\. M-l is dated 8-11-1978, The certificate 
F.x, M-5 has been given on 27-11-1978 by the District Medical 
OITic , Salem. The certificate Ex W-14 is dated 21-12-1978 
within a period of 25 days after Ex. M-5. Hbis certificate is 
given by the skin snecialist attached to the Government Head¬ 
quarters Hospital Salem. The certificate makes it clear that 
this workman Sri Subramani is having Tuberculoid Hansen 
disease on his left Buttock. The skin smear for AFB was 
found to be negative. The doctor certified that this disease 
is non-infec-tual and the patient can do the regular duties 
and he is fit for any type of work. If I may repeat this certi¬ 
ficate that this workman is fit to do anv type of work is 
by specialist dealing with skin diseases like leprosy, 

13. The workman in his anxiety to mainta : n his somce 
of livelihood has gone further and got certificate from the 
Stanley Hospital. That certificate is Ex. W-16. This is 
dated 5-7-1979. This cannot be said to be very far off 
from the date of Ex, M-5. This certificate issued by the 


Civil Assistant Surgeon attached lo the .eprosy section of 
the Government Stanley Hospital, Madras makes it clear that 
this workman Sri Subramani is suffering irom Tuberculoid 
type of Hensen's disease (non-incllective form of leprosy) find

impossible lo discard this certificate issued by the Specialist 
(sxm) of the Hosp.tal dealing with leprosy cases. In fact 
the Distiiet Medical Officer, Salem in his certificate dated 
22-5-1980 has confirmed that the workman is now fit to 
rejoin duty Of course he has indicated that he should 
continue to take the treatment regularly. Therefore these 
ceilificates in favour of the workman that he is fit Lo do 
the job in the Mines of the Respondent-Management Will 
have over-riding effect on the certificates given by MW-1. 
Lx. M-2 and on the certificate Ex. M-5 given by the District 
Medical Officer, Salem particularly because they were not 
specialists in the field of leprosy and MW-1 admitted that 
a specialist in leprosy after examining the patient might 
give different opinion and the leprosy of -the Petitioner G 
not contagious in nature and is quite curable. 

14, The learned counsel appearing for the Management 
argued that after the District Medical Officer has certified 
under Ex. M-5 that this workman Subramani is not fit for the 
work, the remedy available under the terms of Settlement 
Lx. M-l hud become exhausted and it is not open to the 
workman to go further and obtain the certificates from other 
doctors and ihe Management is not bound to consider those 
certificates. It is pertinent to point out that the same stand 
was taken by the management in Ex. W-12 dated 8-8-1980 
addressed to the Assistant Labour Commissioner (Central). 
But one cannot forget that the certificate Ex. M-5 is not by 
a specialist in the field. That apart theie are documents fo 
indicate that the Management had kept the issue open. The 
Management has not chosen to say that Ex. M-5 gave a 
finality to the controversy. In fact, the Standing Order Ex. 
M-9 clearly indicates in clause (6) that any workman, who, 
during the course of his employment is found to be medi¬ 
cally unfit by the Company doctor shall have the right to 
appear before a doctor not below the rank of a Civil Surgeon 
or District Medical Officer and his decision’ shall be accep¬ 
table to the Management. Exs. W-14 and W-16 arc defini¬ 
tely by doctors of Civil Surgeon cadre. Ex, W-l certificate 
is given by WW-1 who was the District Medical Officer when 
he examined this patient and he is a specialist in leprosy. 
That apart Exs. W-7, W-8 and M-8 would confirm that the 
Management had not closed the matter with Ex. M-5 certi¬ 
ficate. Ex. M-8 indicates that the Assistant Labour Com¬ 
missioner (Central) proposed to have a discussion with the 
parties on 28-2T979. Ex. W-7 dated 16-4-1980 a letter front 
the Assistant Commissioner of Labour (Central) to the 
General Secretary of the Union states that the worker was 
advised to approach the District Medical Officer. Ex, W-8 
is a letter by the Union to the District Medical Officer, dated 
5-5-1980, in which the Un on has asked for the certificate 
of the District Medical Officer, who found the workman to 
he fit to join duty. Therefore it is impossible to accept 
the theory of the Management that by reason of the issue of 
Ex. M-5 by the District Medical Officer the remedy available 
under the terms of the settlement has beejr txltiVistcd. In a 
matter like this where the survival of a workman is involved 
it is not just and proper to adopt this technical und inflexible 
attitude. That will completely deny justice to the workman. 
That will also discard the very valuable certificates of fitness 
given by the most competent medical men like Exs, W-14 and 
W-16 wh'ch are in a very close proximity to Ex M-5 
certificate given by the general Medical Practitioner. There¬ 
fore the workman represented by the Union has made out 
a case that he is medically fit to do the work of the Eaith 
Loader in the Mines of the Respondent. Therefoie the 
workman represented by the Union has made out a case that 
lie is medically fit to do the work of the Earth Loader in 
the Mines of the Respondent. Therefore the term'nation 
of the services of the workmen by the Management is not 
legal and has got to be set aside. 

15. Accordingly, an award is passed setting aside the 
order of the Management terminating the services of the 
workman Sri Subramani with effect from 11-8-1978 and the 
workman is ordeied to be reinstated with back wapes from 
the date of reference, namely, 27-2-1982. Theie will be no 
order as to costs.
